Around Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for nilpotent orbits and parabolic Slodowy varieties
Do Kien Hoang, Vasily Krylov, and Dmytro Matvieievskyi

Abstract
. Let G G be a complex reductive algebraic group. In [ LMM21 ] Ivan Losev, Lucas Mason-Brown and the third-named author suggested a symplectic duality between nilpotent Slodowy slices in 𝔤 ∨ \mathfrak{g}^{\vee} and affinizations of certain G G -equivariant covers of special nilpotent orbits. In this paper, we study the various versions of Hikita conjecture for this pair. We show that the original statement of the conjecture does not hold for the pairs in question and propose a refined version. We discuss the general approach towards the proof of the refined Hikita conjecture and prove this refined version for the parabolic Slodowy varieties, which includes many of the cases considered in [ LMM21 ] and more. Applied to the setting of [ LMM21 ] , the refined Hikita conjecture explains the importance of special unipotent ideals from the symplectic duality point of view. We also discuss applications of our results. In the appendices, we discuss some classical questions in Lie theory that relate the refined version and the original version. We also explain how one can use our results to simplify some proofs of known results in the literature. As a combinatorial application of our results we observe an interesting relation between the geometry of Springer fibers and left Kazhdan-Lusztig cells in the corresponding Weyl group.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Symplectic duality

The main and most general objects of interest in this paper are conical symplectic singularities. We give the exact definition and the most important properties in
Section

2.1
, but for now we want to focus on an important special class of examples. For a connected reductive group
G
and a finite-dimensional representation
{\bf{N}}
of
G
Braverman, Finkelberg and Nakajima in
[
Nak16
]
,
[
BFN18
]
gave a mathematical definition of the Coulomb branch of cotangent type
\mathcal{M}_{C}(G,{\bf{N}})
of a
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theory attached to the pair
(G,{\bf{N}})
. In
[
Bel23
]
Bellamy proved that
\mathcal{M}_{C}(G,{\bf{N}})
has symplectic singularities (see also
[
Wee22
]
). In
[
BDFRT02
]
the mathematical definition of a Coulomb branch was extended to the representations of non-cotangent type (under some “anomaly cancelation” condition), and the authors conjectured that the resulting varieties have symplectic singularities. In many cases it is known to be true, which leads to a big class of conical symplectic singularities coming as Coulomb branches of
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theories.

A
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theory also comes with its Higgs branch, in many cases coinciding with the hyper-Kähler quotient
N/\!\!/\!\!/G
. In
[
IS96
]
Intrilligator and Seiberg suggested the “mirror duality” between
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theories that exchange Higgs and Coulomb branches. Thus, in many cases, the Higgs and the Coulomb branches of a
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theory are conical symplectic singularities, and it is natural to expect that their (different) structural properties are governed by the same properties of the gauge theory, and thus are interchanged between each other.

This idea leads to the notion of a symplectic duality, formulated in
[
BPW16
]
,
[
BLPW16
]
(interestingly, the authors of these papers came up with the idea of symplectic duality independently of the works of physicists). Namely, we expect to have many pairs of conical symplectic singularities
(X^{\vee},X)
that have some of their structural properties interchanged, see
Section

2.1
for details. Roughly, one of the expectations is that the Lie algebra of a maximal torus
T_{X}
acting on
X
is interchanged with the space of deformations of
X^{\vee}
and vice versa. One also expects that in cases when both
X
and
X^{\vee}
have symplectic resolutions
Y
,
Y^{\vee}
, there is a (canonical) bijection
Y^{T_{X}}\simeq(Y^{\vee})^{T_{Y^{\vee}}}
and both of these sets are
finite
.

It is important to make two remarks. First, the Higgs branch of a
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theory may not be normal (and therefore cannot be a conical symplectic singularity), and the Coulomb branch may not be conical. Moreover, even in the case of quiver gauge theories where we can define and compute Higgs and Coulomb branches, assuming they are both conical symplectic singularities we cannot prove that the properties of symplectic duality hold, although it is expected to be true.

Second, the definition of symplectic duality makes sense without mentioning
3
-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetric gauge theories. Many examples of expected symplectic dual
(X^{\vee},X)
do not have a known gauge theory that produces
X
and
X^{\vee}
as Higgs and Coulomb branches respectively. Crucially, this includes most of the examples considered in this text. Constructing such a theory is an extremely interesting problem that is, however, not discussed in this paper. In work in progress, Finkelberg, Hanany, and Nakajima realize some but not all of the examples considered in our paper as Higgs and Coulomb branches of orthosymplectic quiver gauge theories, and we are grateful to the authors for explaining their results to us.

1.2.
Parabolic Slodowy slices

In this paper we focus on some specific cases of
(X^{\vee},X)
that are expected to be symplectic dual. Let
G
be a complex semisimple Lie group,
G^{\vee}
be the Langlands dual of
G
, and
\mathfrak{g}
,
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
be the corresponding Lie algebras. Let
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
be a nilpotent element, and
\chi^{\vee}=(e^{\vee},\bullet)\in(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{*}
. Let
S(e^{\vee})\subset\mathfrak{g}
be the Slodowy slice to
e^{\vee}
, and
S(\chi^{\vee})\subset(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{*}
be its image under the identification
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}\simeq(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{*}
given by the Killing form. Consider the intersection
X^{\vee}=S(\chi^{\vee})\cap\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
of
S(\chi^{\vee})
with the nilpotent cone. This is a conical symplectic singularity that admits a symplectic resolution. In
[
LMM21
, Section 9.3]
Losev, Mason-Brown and the third-named author suggest a candidate for a symplectic dual
X
, we recall it in
Section

5
. It generalizes a special case that appeared in the literature before, see, e.g.,
[
BLPW16
, Section 10.2.2]
. Let
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
be a Levi subalgebra containing
e^{\vee}
, and assume that
e^{\vee}
is a regular nilpotent element in
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
. Let
\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be the Langlands dual of
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
,
L\subset G
the corresponding subgroup, and choose a parabolic
P\subset G
with Levi factor
L
. Then
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}(G/P)])
. We note that it admits a symplectic resolution by
T^{*}(G/P)
. Then
X^{\vee}
and
X
are symplectic dual to each other.

An important generalization of the last example is as follows. Let
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be two different parabolic subalgebras, and
\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be the corresponding Levi subalgebras. Consider the generalized Springer resolution map
\rho\colon T^{*}(G/Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Pick a regular nilpotent element
e\in\mathfrak{l}
. We define
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})=\rho^{-1}(S(\chi))
, and let
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})])
to be also denoted by
S(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
.
We have the natural bijection between roots of
\mathfrak{g}
and roots of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. It follows that parabolic subalgebras
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathfrak{g}
determine the parabolic subalgebras
\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
with Levi subalgebras
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{\vee}
respectively.
We expect that
X
is symplectic dual to
X^{\vee}=S(\mathfrak{q}^{\vee},\mathfrak{p}^{\vee})
.
The pair
(X^{\vee},X)
is the main object of our interest.

Let
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
be the space of characters of
\mathfrak{l}
. Let us mention that the morphism
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})\rightarrow S(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
deforms naturally over
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, we will denote this deformation by
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})\rightarrow S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
.

1.3.
Hikita-Nakajima conjectures

We are interested in (different versions of) the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for
(X^{\vee},X)
as above. We explore the general setting in
Section

4.2
, here we focus on the main object of interest. Let
T\subset G
be a maximal torus. The Hikita conjecture
[
Hik17
]
claims the following.

Conjecture 1.1
.

There is a graded algebra isomorphism
H^{*}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\simeq\mathbb{C}[S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{T}]
,

where
S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{T}
are
schematic
fixed points.
In this paper, we consider a more general version of the conjecture, that we call the equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture. Let
Z_{L^{\vee}}
be the center of
L^{\vee}
, and
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the contracting torus acting on
X^{\vee}
. Then we can consider a
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebra
H_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
. On the dual side
X
admits a deformation
S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
over
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, and let
\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(X)
be its quantization over
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Pick a generic cocharacter
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}T
, and let
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(X))
be the Cartan subquotient. Note that it is also a
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebra.

Conjecture 1.2
.

There is a graded isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebras
H_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l},\hbar)}(X))
.

Note that specializing to the point
(0,0)\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}

Conjecture

1.2
recovers
Conjecture

1.1
. Specializing to
\hbar=0
,
Conjecture

1.1
recovers the following conjecture (to be called Hikita-Nakajima conjecture).

Conjecture 1.3
.

There is a graded isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})]
-algebras
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\simeq\mathbb{C}[S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]
.

We finish this section by listing the known results of the Hikita and (equivariant) Hikita-Nakajima conjectures.

The Hikita conjecture was proven for the case of Hilbert scheme of points on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
, parabolic type
A
Slodowy slices and hypertoric varieties in
[
Hik17
, Theorem 1.1, Theorem A.1, Theorem B.1]
. In
[
Shl20
]
, Shlykov has proven the case of
\mathbb{A}^{2}/\Gamma
where
\Gamma
is a finite subgroup of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
In
[
KTWWY19
, Theorem 1.5]
, Kamnitzer, Tingley, Webster, Weekes and Yacobi have proven Hikita conjecture for the ADE slices in the affine Grassmanian.
They have also proven the equivariant version for type A quivers. Moreover, the equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture in the case of ADE slices in the affine Grassmanian that admit a symplectic resolution can be deduced from the results of Kamnitzer, Tingley, Webster, Weekes and Yacobi (see
[
KTWWY19
, Section 8.3]
,
[
KTWWY19a
, Section 1.3 and Theorem 1.5]
, see also
[
Kam22
, Section 6.6]
). The results of
[
Shl20
]
were recently generalized to the equivariant and quantum case in
[
CHY24
]
. In
[
Set24
]
Setiabrata studies Hikita conjecture for
\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}/\!/\!/T
. In
[
KS
]
, Shlykov and the second author proved the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for ADHM spaces (also known as Gieseker varieties) and initiated the approach towards the proof of Hikita-Nakajima conjecture that we will be using in this paper.

1.4.
Main results

Below, we list the main results obtained in this paper and discuss the structure of the text. The first important new result is obtained in
Section

6
. Namely, we claim the following.

Proposition 1.4
.

The Conjectures
1.1
and
1.2
do not hold as stated for the pair

X^{\vee}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})]),~X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})]).

In fact we construct explicit counterexamples when
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{b}
, i.e.
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}(G/P)])
. For
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
a regular nilpotent let
\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee})=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{b}^{\vee})
. The key issues with Conjectures
1.1
,
1.2
are as follows, see
Section

6
for details.

•

The algebra
H_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee}))
is free over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
, while the algebra
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(X))
may have
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-torsion.

•

In many cases the algebra
\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]
is cyclic indecomposable over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]
while
H^{*}(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee}))
is not indecomposable in general.

To remedy these issues in
Section

4.2
, we propose
Conjecture

8.4
as a replacement of the equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture in a general setting. For the pair

X^{\vee}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})]),~X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})])

it leads to
Proposition

9.4
and
Theorem

9.5
, that are the main results of the paper. Let us start with the case
\hbar=0
.

1.4.1.
Refined Hikita-Nakajima for
\hbar=0

For
\hbar=0
, we are comparing the following algebras:

H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))~\text{vs}~\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}].

Note that we have the natural embedding

(1.4.1)

H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\hookrightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}})

and the latter algebra is just the direct sum of polynomial algebras labeled by the (finite) set of fixed points
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}_{-},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}_{-})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}
that we describe explicitly in Appendix
E
. By localization theorem, the embedding (
1.4.1
) is an
isomorphism generically
over
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\operatorname{pt})
. Moreover, we have a natural restriction homomorphism

(1.4.2)

H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))

which is
generically surjective
. We propose to replace the algebra
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
appearing in the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture by the
image

(1.4.3)

\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})).

Note that the fiber of the image (
1.4.3
) over a
generic
point
\lambda\in\operatorname{Spec}H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\operatorname{pt})
is
equal
to the fiber of
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
at
\lambda
(use generic surjectivity of (
1.4.2
)). So, considered as a coherent sheaf on
\operatorname{Spec}H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\operatorname{pt})
, it has rank equal to the number of fixed points
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}
. It then follows from Nakayama lemma that the fibers of the image (
1.4.3
) over arbitrary
\lambda
have dimension
at least

|\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}|
. So, our construction does not replace
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
by some “smaller” sheaf of algebras, it rather modifies the sheaf
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
at the special points
\lambda\in\operatorname{Spec}H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\operatorname{pt})
.
Note, in particular, it is
not
true in general that the fiber of the image (
1.4.3
) at
\lambda
is equal to the image
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})_{\lambda}\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})_{\lambda}
. Of course, we have a surjective map in one direction, but it might
not
be an isomorphism. In other words, there are elements in the fiber of (
1.4.3
) that can
not
be obtained as images of the elements of
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})_{\lambda}
. These elements should rather be thought as “limits” of the images of the elements of
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})_{\lambda^{\prime}}
for generic
\lambda^{\prime}
as
\lambda^{\prime}
goes to
\lambda
.

So, we replace the LHS of the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture with the image (
1.4.3
). Let us now describe what we do with the RHS.

The analog of the map (
1.4.2
) for the RHS is the pull-back homomorphism

(1.4.4)

\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]

induced by the natural morphism

S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})

(recall that
S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
is the affinization of
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
, which, by the definition, is the preimage of
S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})
under the natural morphism
T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
). One can show that the sources in (
1.4.2
), (
1.4.4
) are isomorphic, i.e., the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture holds for the pair
(X^{\vee}=T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee},\,X=\widetilde{S}(\chi))
. So, (
1.4.4
) is indeed a reasonable analog of (
1.4.2
).

One can show that the morphism (
1.4.4
) is generically surjective, so the natural guess for the replacement of the RHS of the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture would be taking the image of (
1.4.4
). This is
almost
the case. Namely, the algebra
\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]
is
not
torsion free over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})]
in general but the algebra
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
is. To remedy this inconsistency, we replace
\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]
by its image under the natural pullback morphism

(1.4.5)

\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}].

This morphism should be compared with the (injective) morphism (
1.4.1
). Note that the morphism (
1.4.1
) is an
isomorphism generically
. Summarizing, our replacement for the RHS of the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture is the image:

\operatorname{Im}(\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]).

As before, this image is a natural modification of the original algebra
\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]
coinciding with it at the generic point.

So, for
\hbar=0
, our main result is the following.

Theorem 1.5
.

There is an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})]
-algebras:

\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Im}(\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]).

For the statement of the main theorem and details we refer to the main text, here we limit ourselves to expressing the main idea and illustrating it for
\hbar=0
.

1.4.2.
Refined equivariant Hikita-Nakakjima

The general case is similar to the
\hbar=0
situation. The LHS of
Theorem

1.5
is replaced by
\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})))
. The replacement of the RHS can be described as follows. First of all, the map
S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})
quantizes to the homomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p}))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))
inducing the homomorphism
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))
quantizing (
1.4.4
). The pull-back map (
1.4.5
) is quantized as follows. We consider the
sheaf
quantization
\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))
of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})}
and then (
1.4.5
) is quantized by the natural map

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))\rightarrow\Gamma(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))).

So, the main result of the paper is the following theorem.

Theorem 1.6
.

There is an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebras:

(1.4.6)

\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Im}(\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))\rightarrow\Gamma(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))))).

One possible way to think about Theorem
1.6
is the following.
Both algebras
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{*}\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
and
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}({S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))
have a natural action of the “big” (polynomial) algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
coming from the embedding
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})\subset T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
and the quantization of the map
S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}S(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})
. Assuming torsion freeness of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{b})))
over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
, our refined equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture claims that the subalgebras of
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
and
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(S(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{b})))
generated by
1
under the action of the algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
are isomorphic.

Let us also briefly mention the idea of the proof of
Theorem

1.6
(cf.
[
KS
]
). Recall that symplectic duality predicts the existence of (canonical) bijection

(1.4.7)

Y^{T_{X}}\simeq(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}}.

The existence of the bijection (
1.4.7
) can be checked in our example
Y=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
,
Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})
and follows from the results of Appendix
E.2
. Now, the idea is to realize both of the algebras appearing in
Theorem

1.6
as
subalgebras
of the direct sum
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]^{\oplus|Y^{T_{X}}|}
. The desired embedding of the LHS of (
1.4.6
) is given by composing
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))

with the pull-back (
1.4.1
) to the torus fixed points
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\hookrightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}})
. The algebra in the RHS of (
1.4.6
) is obviously a subalgebra of
\Gamma(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))))
. It them follows from the results of
[
BLPW16
, Section 5]
(see also
[
Los17
, Section 5.2]
) that we have an isomorphism

\Gamma(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))))\simeq\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]^{|Y^{T_{X}}|}.

Now, to prove
Theorem

1.6
we need to identify the images of

(1.4.8)

H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}),\,\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar}(S(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))

inside the direct sum of polynomial algebras. We then note that both of the algebras in (
1.4.8
) are naturally quotients of the same algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
. So, now we just have two maps
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightrightarrows\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]^{\oplus|Y^{T_{X}}|}
and our goal is to prove that these maps are equal. The key point is that this can already be done at generic
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}
and reduces to the standard computations. Namely, on the LHS of the conjecture, this is just the computation of weights of
G
-equivariant bundles on
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
at the torus fixed points. On the RHS, this is the computation of the highest weights of irreducible modules in the regular integral block of the category
\mathcal{O}
for parabolic
W
-algebras.

1.4.3.
Comparison of refined Hikita-Nakajima and Hikita-Nakajima conjectures

The connection between our theorem and the original Hikita conjecture is worth elaborating on. First of all, for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{b}
, i.e., for the pair
(X^{\vee}=T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee},X=\widetilde{S}(\chi))
, our Theorem
9.5
implies the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture without any modifications. The situation is much more complicated in general.
Specifically,
Theorem

9.5
along with certain additional conditions (see Section
8.4
) would imply that
Conjecture

1.2
and
Conjecture

1.1
. These conditions are briefly discussed in Section
10
and thoroughly examined in
[
H24a
]
. In fact, these conditions are necessary conditions for
Conjecture

1.1
to hold (
[
H24a
, Propositions 1.1, 1.5]
). Therefore, our main result can essentially be viewed as a precise replacement, rather than a weaker version, of the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for nilpotent orbits. For
\mathfrak{g}
of type A,
Theorem

9.5
uniformly reproduces all type A theorems on the Hikita conjecture (Appendix
D
). This includes recent results from
[
Bru08
]
,
[
KTWWY19
]
, as well as classical results from
[
DP81
]
,
[
Tan82
]
. Our theorem also uniformly generalizes other results for general
\mathfrak{g}
obtained in
[
Car86
]
,
[
KP12
]
, and
[
CK17
]
.

1.5.
Motivations and applications

1.5.1.
Connection to unipotent characters

An important motivation for the Hikita conjecture in the case
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{b}
comes from
[
LMM21
, Section 9.3]
. In the loc.cit. it is shown how a slightly stronger version of
Conjecture

1.1
explains the appearance of special unipotent central characters in the study of canonical quantizations of orbit covers. Namely, the following diagram was expected to be commutative.

\textstyle{H^{*}_{Z(L^{\vee})\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{res}
\textstyle{H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))}

As mentioned already, there is no isomorphism
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))
in general. However, the commutative diagram of
Proposition

9.4
is sufficient for the argument in the paper. Similarly, in the case when
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
is not regular,
Conjecture

8.4
is sufficient for the argument in
[
LMM21
, Section 9.3]
. Understanding the conjecture for non-regular in Levi
e^{\vee}
is a topic of an ongoing work in progress of the authors. We refer to
Section

10.4
for the discussion.

1.5.2.
Geometry of Springer fibers and Kazhdan-Lusztig cells

Theorem

9.5
offers a bridge between representation theory and geometry. In particular, the set-theoretic support of the specialization of the image of the “big” algebra action on
1\in\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})))
at a parameter
(\lambda,t)\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
records the highest weight of certain irreducible
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
-modules annihilated by the same primitive ideal. Compared with the cohomological side, we obtain a new, highly nontrivial relation between the geometry of Springer fibers and the theory of Kazhdan-Lusztig cells. This relation is mentioned in
Section

10.3
and is part of a work in progress.

1.6.
Structure of the paper

We finish the section by explaining the structure of the paper. First, we recall the already existing in the literature results that are important for our paper. Section
2
focuses on conical symplectic singularities and their deformation. Section
3
recalls the basics of equivariant cohomology and provides explicit computations of fixed points of Slodowy varieties. In Section
4
we fix the settings of the symplectic duality adopted in the paper. Section
5
introduces the main symplectic dual pairs considered in the paper following
[
LMM21
]
and
[
BLPW16
]
. The first new results appear in Section
6
, where we analyze the Hikita conjecture for Slodowy slices and nilpotent orbits and provide explicit counterexamples. Section
7
is quite technical and provides the necessary background for understanding the highest weights of parabolic
W
-algebras used in the proof of the main result. In Section
8
, we state the main conjecture (
Conjecture

8.4
) that serves as our replacement of the equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture in the literature and discuss the differences between the two. Section
9
states and proves the main result of the paper (
Theorem

9.5
). In Section
10
, we discuss the corollaries of the results obtained and potential directions for further study. Finally, we have 6 appendices that fix notations and provide explicit computations used in the paper.

1.7.
Acknowledgements

This paper was made possible thanks to the feedback and constant sharing of the ideas from many mathematicians, their help is extremely appreciated. We are especially grateful to Roman Bezrukavnikov, Alexander Braverman, Pavel Etingof, Andrei Ionov, Joel Kamnitzer, Ivan Losev, Hiraku Nakajima, Peng Shan, Ben Webster, and Alex Weekes.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Conical symplectic singularities

Let
X
be a normal Poisson scheme. Following Beauville
[
Bea00
]
, we say that
X
has
symplectic singularities
if

(i)

The regular (smooth) locus
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
admits a symplectic form
\omega
.

(ii)

There is a resolution of singularities
\pi\colon Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
, such that
\pi^{*}\omega
extends to a regular (not necessarily symplectic)
2
-form on
Y
.

We say that a graded normal Poisson scheme
X
is a
conical symplectic singularities
if
X
has symplectic singularities and admits a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action, contracting
X
onto a point. Note that a conical symplectic singularity is automatically affine. We will call
Y\rightarrow X
a
symplectic resolution
of
X
if the extension of
\pi^{*}\omega
to
Y
is the
symplectic
form. Let us list some examples relevant to this paper, see
Section

2.3
and
Section

2.4
for more details.

Example
.

(a)

The Springer resolution
T^{*}\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{N}
is a symplectic resolution and thus satisfies (ii). More generally, for arbitrary Parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
, the affinization morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon Y:=T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow X=\operatorname{Spec}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
is a conical symplectic resolution.

(b)

Let
G
and
\mathcal{N}
be as above, and let
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathcal{N}
be a nilpotent orbit. Then
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}])
is a conical symplectic singularity,
[
Pan91
]
. Moreover, if
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is a nilpotent cover,
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
is a conical symplectic singularity,
[
Los21
, Lemma 2.5]
. Note that it, in general, may not possess a symplectic resolution. We refer interested readers to
[
Fu03a
]
and
[
Fu03
]
.

(c)

Let
X=S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}
be the intersection of the Slodowy slice to
\chi
with the nilpotent cone. Then
Y=\widetilde{S}(\chi)\rightarrow S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}=X
is a conical symplectic resolution, where the map
\pi\colon\widetilde{S}(\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}
is given by restricting the Springer resolution to
X\subset\mathcal{N}
.

(d)

Generalizing
(a)
and
(c)
, we can take
Y=\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
to be the parabolc Slodowy variety, and
X:=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})]
.

Suppose that
X
has symplectic singularities. In the remainder of the section we introduce some of the key objects associated with
X
.

2.1.1.
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations

The following was proved in
[
Los19
, Prop 2.1]
following
[
BCHM10
]
, see also
[
Nam08
, A.7]
.

Proposition 2.1
.

There is a birational projective Poisson morphism
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
such that

(i)

\widetilde{X}
has symplectic singularities;

(ii)

Every Weil divisor on
\widetilde{X}
has a (nonzero) integer multiple that is Cartier, i.e.
\widetilde{X}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial;

(iii)

\codim_{\widetilde{X}}(\widetilde{X}\setminus\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}})\geq 4
;

(iv)

If
X
is conical symplectic singularity, then
\widetilde{X}
admits a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action, such that
\pi
is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant.

We say that
\widetilde{X}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
of
X
.

It is important to note that
X
usually admits multiple
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalizations. In what follows, we fix a choice of
\widetilde{X}
.

2.1.2.
Namikawa space

Define the
Namikawa space

\mathfrak{h}_{X}=H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
. The key property of
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
is that it classifies Poisson deformations of
\widetilde{X}
. Namely, we have the following result.

Proposition 2.2
.

[
Los22
, Proposition 2.6]

There is a graded Poisson deformation
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
of
\widetilde{X}
over
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
, such that for any graded Poisson deformation
\widetilde{X}_{B}
over
\Spec(B)
there is a unique
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant morphism
\Spec(B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{h}_{X}
, such that
\widetilde{X}_{B}\simeq\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\times_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\Spec(B)
.

We call the deformation
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{h}_{X}
the
universal Poisson deformation
of
\widetilde{X}
. The variety
X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}])
is a graded Poisson deformation of
X
over
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
. We note that
X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{h}_{X}
is not universal in the sense of
Proposition

2.2
, but it admits a surjective finite map to the universal Poisson deformation of
X
. For the purposes of this paper, we prefer working with
X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
over the universal Poisson deformation of
X
.

2.1.3.
Automorphisms of
X

We finish this section with a short discussion of the automorphisms of
X
. Consider the group of Poisson automorphisms of
X
commuting with the contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action, and let
Z_{X}
be its maximal reductive quotient.

Warning 2.3
.

Note that
Z_{X}
may be disconnected.

Let
T_{X}\subset Z_{X}
be the maximal torus. The action of
Z_{X}
on
X
extends to the action of
Z_{X}
on
X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
, and similarly the action of
T_{X}
on
X
lifts to an action on
\widetilde{X}
and extends to an action on
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
. We denote the Lie algebra of
T_{X}
by
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
. In
Section

2.4
, we describe the Namikawa space
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
and the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
for the nilpotent Slodowy slice
X=S_{\chi}
and the affinization of the cotangent bundle of partial flag variety
X=T^{*}\mathcal{P}
. These spaces play an important role in what follows.

2.1.4.
Chambers

Let us now discuss stratifications of the spaces
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
,
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
into the union of certain
chambers
separated by certain
walls
. These stratifications will play an important role in the paper.

Recall the morphism
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\rightarrow X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
. Consider the set
\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
of all
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
such that
\widetilde{X}_{\chi}
is not affine, i.e. the map
\widetilde{X}_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X_{\chi}
is not an isomorphism. Irreducible components of
\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
define walls, and thus, we obtain a division of
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
into chambers.

The following proposition holds by the results of Namikawa (see
[
Nam15
, Main Theorem]
and footnote
2
in loc.cit.). Let
W
be the Namikawa-Weyl group (it was introduced in
[
Nam10
]
see also
[
Los22
, Section 2.3]
). For a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
we will denote by
\operatorname{Amp}(\widetilde{X})
the ample cone of
X
.

Proposition 2.4
.

The set
\mathfrak{h}_{X}\setminus\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{sing}}_{X}
is equal to the union
\bigsqcup_{\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X,\,w\in W_{X}}w(\operatorname{Amp}(\widetilde{X}))
. Every irreducible component of
\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{sing}}
is a codimension one linear subspace of
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
.

Let us now describe a natural stratification of
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
into chambers separated by certain walls.
We assume that
X
admits a symplectic resolution
Y
such that the set
Y^{T_{X}}
is finite.

Let
\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{X}
be the subset of
x\in\mathfrak{t}_{X}
such that
Y^{x}=Y^{T_{X}}
, where by
Y^{x}
we mean zeroes of the vector field on
Y
defined by
x\in\mathfrak{t}_{X}=\operatorname{Lie}T_{X}
. Clearly,
\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
can be explicitly described as follows. Let
\Delta_{X}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{*}
be the set of all
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
-weights that appear in
T_{p}Y
for
y\in Y^{T_{X}}
. For
\alpha\in\Delta_{X}
let
H_{\alpha}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{X}
be the hyperplane given by the equation
\langle\alpha,-\rangle=0
. It is easy to see that

\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Delta_{X}}H_{\alpha}.

Remark 2.5
.

There should be an internal definition of
\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
that does not involve a choice of
Y
and works for arbitrary conical symplectic singularity
X
(not necessarily having a symplectic resolution). One candidate is the set
\Delta^{\prime}_{X}
defined as follows (see
[
KP21
, Section 3.1]
). Let
\mathbb{C}[X]_{+}\subset\mathbb{C}[X]
be the ideal generated by the elements of positive degree w.r.t. the contracting action. Then
\Delta^{\prime}_{X}
is the set of
T_{X}
-weights of
\mathbb{C}[X]/(\mathbb{C}[X]_{+})^{2}
. In other words,
\Delta^{\prime}_{X}
is the set of
T_{X}
-weights of the cotangent space of
X
at the unique
T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-fixed point. It is expected that
\Delta_{X}=\Delta^{\prime}_{X}
when
X
has a symplectic resolution.

2.1.5.
Equivariant formality

Fix a symplectic singularity
X
and assume that it admits a symplectic resolution
Y
. It in principle follows from the results of Kaledin that
H^{*}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y)
is free over
H^{*}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
. On the other hand, if
Y^{T_{X}}
is finite, then this claim becomes extremely easy. Since all of the varieties
Y
considered in this paper will have isolated
T_{X}
-fixed points we have decided to include the following lemma.

Lemma 2.6
.

Assume that
Y\rightarrow X
is a conical symplectic resolution s.t.
Y^{T_{X}}
is finite. Then
H^{*}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y)
is free over
H^{*}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Proof.

It is enough to construct a finite
T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant paving of
Y
. Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
be a generic cocharacter of
T_{X}
.
Consider a cocharacter
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
given by
t\mapsto(\nu(t),t^{N})
for
N\gg 0
. Then, the induced action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
contracts
Y
to the finite set
Y^{T_{X}}
. Taking attractors to fixed points and using Bialynicki-Birula Theorem (see
[
Bia73
, Section 4]
), we obtain the desired affine paving.
∎

2.2.
Nilpotent orbits

Let
G
be a complex simple group with a fixed maximal torus
T\subset G
. Let
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}G
,
\mathfrak{h}=\operatorname{Lie}T
be the corresponding Lie algebras. The variety
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
admits a natural Poisson structure induced by the commutator on
\mathfrak{g}
, and the symplectic leaves of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
are coadjoint orbits. The coadjoint orbit
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is
nilpotent
if it is stable under the scaling action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. We write
\text{$\mathchar 525$}(G)
for the set of nilpotent coadjoint
G
-orbits. Let
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
denote the union of all nilpotent coadjoint orbits. In classical types, we have a combinatorial parameterization of nilpotent orbits. Namely, for a positive integer
n
let
\mathcal{P}(n)
be the set of partitions of
n
, i.e. non-increasing sequences of positive integers
\mathbf{p}=(\mathbf{p}_{1},\mathbf{p}_{2},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{k})
, such that
n=\sum_{i}\mathbf{p}_{i}
. For an odd number
n
, we say that a partition
\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{P}(n)
is of type
B
if every even member of
\mathbf{p}
comes with an even multiplicity, and denote the set of type
B
partitions of
n
by
\mathcal{P}_{B}(n)
. Similarly, for even
n
, we say that a partition
\mathbf{p}
is of type
C
(resp. of type
D
) if every odd (resp. even) member of
\mathbf{p}
comes with an even multiplicity and denote the corresponding set of partitions by
\mathcal{P}_{C}(n)
(resp.
\mathcal{P}_{D}(n)
). A partition
\mathbf{p}\in\mathcal{P}_{D}(n)
is called very even if all members of
\mathbf{p}
are even.

Proposition 2.7

(Section 5.1,
[
CM93
]
)
.

Suppose
G
is a simple group of classical type. Then the following are true:

(a)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
, then there is a bijection:

\text{$\mathchar 525$}(G)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}(n)

(b)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}
, then there is a bijection:

\text{$\mathchar 525$}(G)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}_{B}(2n+1)

(c)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
, then there is a bijection:

\text{$\mathchar 525$}(G)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}_{C}(2n)

(d)

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{2n}
, then there is a surjection:

\text{$\mathchar 525$}(G)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{D}(2n)

Over very even partitions, this map is two-to-one, and over all other partitions, it is a bijection.

In what follows, we write
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}
for the partition corresponding to
\mathbf{p}
under the map above. For
\mathbf{p}
very even we denote the corresponding preimages by
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}^{I}
and
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}^{II}
. If we claim a statement that is true for both
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}^{I}
and
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}^{II}
, we often omit the numerals and refer to the orbit as
\mathbb{O}_{\mathbf{p}}
.

To classify nilpotent orbits of exceptional simple groups we use the Bala-Carter classification. These ideas are also incredibly useful for the classical groups, we recall them below.

Let
L\subset G
be a Levi subgroup containing the torus
T
, and
\mathbb{O}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{*}
be a nilpotent orbit. The nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}=G\cdot\mathbb{O}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is called
saturated
from the pair
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})
and is denoted by
\Sat_{L}^{G}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. An orbit that cannot be saturated from any proper Levi
L\subset G
is called
distinguished
.

Proposition 2.8
.

[
BC76
]
,
[
CM93
, Thm. 8.1.1]

Let
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be a nilpotent orbit. There is a unique up to
G
-conjugation pair
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})
of a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
, and a distinguished orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{*}
, such that
\mathbb{O}=\Sat_{L}^{G}\mathbb{O}_{L}
.

2.3.
Nilpotent orbit covers and birational induction

Recall that
L\subset G
is a Levi subgroup, and
\mathbb{O}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{l}^{*}
is a nilpotent orbit. Pick a parabolic group
P\subset G
with a Levi factor
L
and Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}
. Let
\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}=(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Form a
G
-equivariant fiber bundle
G\times^{P}(\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
over
\mathcal{P}=G/P
and note that the left action of
G
induces a
G
-equivariant moment map

(2.3.1)

G\times^{P}(\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\qquad(g,\xi)\mapsto g\xi.

By
[
LS79
]
, the image of (
2.3.1
) is the closure of a single nilpotent
G
-orbit
\mathbb{O}
, and the orbit
\mathbb{O}
is independent of the choice of parabolic
P
. The correspondence associating to an orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
the orbit
\mathbb{O}
is called
Lusztig-Spaltenstein induction
. We say that
\mathbb{O}
is induced from
(L,\mathbb{O}_{L})
and denote it by
\Ind_{L}^{G}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. We note that the map
G\times^{P}(\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbb{O}}
is finite, but may not be birational. We also remark that an analog of
Proposition

2.8
doesn’t hold for the Lusztig-Spaltenstein induction. To fix these issues we work with birational induction of nilpotent orbit covers.

A
nilpotent cover
is a finite étale
G
-equivariant connected cover of a nilpotent coadjoint orbit. Naturally, if
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is a nilpotent orbit, and
\chi\in\mathbb{O}
, the nilpotent covers of
\mathbb{O}
are in a natural correspondence with the subgroups of
\pi_{1}^{G}(\mathbb{O})=Z_{G}(\chi)/Z_{G}(\chi)^{\circ}
.

Recall that
L\subset G
is a Levi, and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
be a nilpotent cover of
\mathbb{O}_{L}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Similarly to the above, we form a fiber bundle
G\times^{P}(\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}])\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})
, and it admits a moment map
\pi\colon G\times^{P}(\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}])\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
with the image of
\pi
being the closure of the orbit
\mathbb{O}=\Ind_{L}^{G}\mathbb{O}_{L}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{O})
. Then
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is connected, and therefore a nilpotent cover of
\mathbb{O}
. We say that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is
birationally induced
from
(L,\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L})
and denote it by
\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. By
[
LMM21
, Proposition 2.4.1(i)]
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is independent of the choice of the parabolic
P
. A nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
that cannot be induced from a proper Levi subgroup
L
is called
birationally rigid
.

Proposition 2.9
.

[
LMM21
, Proposition 2.4.1(iii)]

For any nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
there is a unique up to
G
-conjugation pair
(L,\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L})
of a Levi subgroup
L\subset G
and a birationally rigid nilpotent cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
, such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
.

2.4.
Parabolic Slodowy varieties

2.4.1.
Partial flag varieties and their deformations

Recall that
P\subset G
is a parabolic subgroup containing
T
with a Levi factor
L
, and
\mathcal{P}=G/P
is the partial flag variety (we will sometimes identify it with the space of parabolic subgroups
P^{\prime}\subset G
that are conjugate to
P
). Consider the symplectic variety
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
that can be canonically identified with
G\times^{P}\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}
. We have the natural morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{O}}
(c.f. (
2.3.1
) for
\mathbb{O}_{L}=\{0\}
).

Remark 2.10
.

As we have mentioned in Section
2.3
, the morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is finite but not birational in general. On the other hand, it factors as
T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}\mathcal{P}])\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, and the first morphism is birational. Moreover,
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}\mathcal{P}])
is nothing else but
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}]
for the cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}
of the orbit
\mathbb{O}
.

Let
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
. The Namikawa space
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
by definition equals to
H^{2}(T^{*}\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})\simeq H^{2}(\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})
. The latter is identified with
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})=(\mathfrak{l}/[\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}])^{*}=(\mathfrak{p}/[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}])^{*}
.
The universal deformation of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
over the base
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
is given as follows:

T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}=G\times^{P}[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}]^{\perp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),~(g,\xi)\mapsto\xi|_{\mathfrak{p}}.

For
P=B
,
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{h})=\mathfrak{h}^{*}
,
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}\mathcal{B}
is the well-known
Grothendieck-Springer resolution
that will be denoted by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
.

For future applications, we will need a description of ample line bundles on
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
. Let
\Delta^{\vee}
be the set of coroots of
\mathfrak{g}
, equivalently, the set of roots of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
.
Let
\Delta^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{p}}\subset\Delta^{\vee}
be the subset consisting of
\beta^{\vee}
such that
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}_{\beta^{\vee}}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}
.
We will say that
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is
\mathfrak{p}
-antidominant
if
\langle\alpha,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\notin\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
for any
\beta^{\vee}\in\Delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}
.
We say that
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is
\mathfrak{l}
-
regular
if
\langle\alpha,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\neq 0
for any
\beta^{\vee}\in\Delta^{\vee}\setminus\Delta_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\vee}
.
We say that
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is
integral
if
\langle\lambda,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}
for any
\beta^{\vee}\in\Delta^{\vee}
.

To every
integral

\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
we can associate the corresponding
P
-character to be denoted by the same symbol
\lambda\colon P\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Let
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}
be the corresponding one-dimensional representation of
P
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda):=G\times^{P}\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}
be the induced line bundle.

The following lemma is standard.

Lemma 2.11
.

Line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda)
is ample iff
\lambda
is
\mathfrak{p}
-antidominant and
\mathfrak{l}
-regular.

For the future applications in this paper, it is useful to know the fiber
T^{*}_{\xi}\mathcal{P}
of the universal deformation of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
over the point
\xi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Let
M=Z_{G}(\xi)
be a Levi subgroup of
G
containing
L
, and let
P_{M}=P\cap M
and
\mathcal{P}_{M}
be the corresponding parabolic subgroup and partial flag variety for
M
respectively. We claim the following.

Lemma 2.12
.

There is an isomorphism
T^{*}_{\xi}\mathcal{P}\simeq G\times^{M}(\xi\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M})
. In particular, for
\mathfrak{l}
-regular
\xi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
we have
T^{*}_{\xi}\mathcal{P}\simeq G/L=G\xi
.

Proof.

Let
Q\supset P
be a parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi factor
\mathfrak{m}
, and let
U\subset Q
be the unipotent radical. Let
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\subset\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
be the subset of all
\eta\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
such that
Z_{G}(\eta)\subset M
. We have

T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}}\mathcal{P}=G\times^{P}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp})\simeq G\times^{Q}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})

We claim that
G\times^{Q}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})\simeq G\times^{M}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M})
. Consider the tautological map
\tau:G\times^{M}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}G\times^{Q}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp})
given by
(g,\eta,x)\mapsto(g,\eta,x)
. We have
G\times^{M}(\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M})\simeq G\times^{Q}(U\times\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M})
. The isomorphism
U\times\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M}\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}\times T^{*}\mathcal{P}_{M}\times\mathfrak{q}^{\perp}
given by
(u,\eta,x)\mapsto u.(\eta,x)
implies that
\tau
is an isomorphism. Specializing to the point
\xi\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{\circ}
, we get the desired statement.
∎

Note that we have the closed embedding

T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}\times\mathfrak{g}^{*}
given by
(g,\xi)\mapsto(gPg^{-1},g\xi)
and inducing the identification:

T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\{(\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\,|\,\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P},\,\xi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}^{*},\,\xi^{\prime}|_{[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}]}=0\}.

The map
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
constructed above, and the composition
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}\times\mathfrak{g}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
give rise to the map
\widetilde{\pi}_{\mathfrak{p}}:T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
that will be important in what follows.

We finish the discussion of partial flag varieties with the discussion of the group
Z_{X}
. As mentioned in
Warning

2.3
, the group
Z_{X}
may be disconnected. For our purposes, it is enough to consider the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{z}_{X}
of
Z_{X}
. Recall that
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
for a
G
-equivariant cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
of a nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathcal{N}
. The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{z}_{X}
in this case was studied by Brylinski and Kostant in
[
BK94
]
, which led to the concept of
shared orbits
, corresponding to the case when
\mathfrak{z}_{X}
is not equal to
\mathfrak{g}
. All shared orbits were classified in recent work
[
FJLS23
]
of Fu, Juteau, Levy, and Sommers. We summarize the results of these two papers in our context as follows.

Proposition 2.13
.

Let
P\subset G
be a parabolic subgroup, and
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}\mathcal{P}])
. Let
L
be the Levi factor of
P
. Then
\mathfrak{z}_{X}\not\simeq\mathfrak{g}
if and only if
\mathfrak{g}\simeq\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(2n+1)
and
\mathfrak{l}\simeq\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(n)
. In this case
\mathfrak{z}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(2n+2)
.

2.4.2.
Slodowy varieties

Let
G
,
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathbb{O}
be as above. Pick a Borel subgroup
B\subset G
and let
\mathcal{B}:=G/B
. Fix a nondegenerate
\mathfrak{g}
-invariant form
(-,-)
on
\mathfrak{g}
. Pick a point
\chi\in\mathbb{O}
, and let
e\in\mathfrak{g}
be the nilpotent element such that
\chi=(e,-)
. Choose an
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{2}
-triple
(e,h,f)
, and let
S(e):=e+\Ker(\ad f)
. Let
\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{g}(i)
be the decomposition into the weight spaces of
[h,-]\curvearrowright\mathfrak{g}
, and consider the Kazhdan
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
\mathfrak{g}
given on
x\in\mathfrak{g}(i)
by
t\cdot x=t^{-2+i}x
. This action restricts to the action on
S(e)
, contracting it onto the point
e
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. We will denote by
S(\chi)
the image of
S(e)
under the isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
given by the form
(-,-)
above.
Variety
S(\chi)
is called the
Slodowy slice
to the nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}
at point
\chi
.

Remark 2.14
.

After the identification
\mathfrak{g}\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, the action above is given by
t\cdot\eta=t^{-2-i}\eta
for
\eta\in\left(\mathfrak{g}(i)\right)^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

We have the natural morphism
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}/\!/G
induced by the embedding
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]^{G}\subset\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]
. Recall also that by the Chevalley restriction theorem,
\mathfrak{g}^{*}/\!/G\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W
, where
W=N(T)/T
is the Weyl group of
(G,T)
. So, we obtain the morphism
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W
inducing the morphism
S(\chi)\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W
.

Set
S_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi):=S(\chi)\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{h}^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
and let
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
be the preimage of
S(\chi)\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
under the Grothendieck-Springer morphism
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. We will also consider the fiber of
S_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}
over zero to be denoted by
\widetilde{S}(\chi)
and called
Slodowy variety
.
Note that
\widetilde{S}(\chi)
is nothing else but the preimage of
S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}
under the Springer morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{b}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
. In particular,
\widetilde{S}(\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}
is a symplectic resolution. Let
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}\subset\widetilde{S}(\chi)
be the preimage
\pi_{\mathfrak{b}}^{-1}(\chi)
, it will be called the
Springer fiber
(corresponding to
\chi
). The natural projection map
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is the graded Poisson deformation of
\widetilde{S}(\chi)
over
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Remark 2.15
.

In most cases
H^{2}(\widetilde{S}(\chi),\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, and
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is in fact the universal Poisson deformation of
\widetilde{S}(\chi)
. When the isomorphism above does not hold, either
\chi
is a regular nilpotent element, or the Slodowy variety
\widetilde{S}(\chi)
can be realized as the Slodowy variety
\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\prime})
for a nilpotent element
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\prime*}
for some simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime*}
, such that
\rk\mathfrak{g}^{\prime*}>\rk\mathfrak{g}
, and
H^{2}(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\prime}),\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\prime*}
. We refer interested readers to
[
LNS12
]
for more details.

We will need an alternative definition of
S(\chi)
. Namely, we will realize it as a Hamiltonian reduction of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
by a certain unipotent subgroup
U_{\ell}\subset G
depending on some vector space
\ell\subset\mathfrak{g}(-1)
(main reference is
[
GG02
]
, note that our
U_{\ell}
is their
M_{\ell}
).

There is a a nondegenerate skew-symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}(-1)
sending a pair
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{g}(-1)\times\mathfrak{g}(-1)
to
\chi([x,y])
. Fix an isotropic subspace
\ell\subset\mathfrak{g}(-1)
, and let

\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}=\ell\oplus\bigoplus_{i\leqslant-2}\mathfrak{g}(i),~\mathfrak{n}_{\ell}=\ell^{\perp}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\leqslant-1}\mathfrak{g}(i).

Remark 2.16
.

Note that for
\ell=0
we have
u_{0}=\mathfrak{u}=\bigoplus_{i\leqslant-2}\mathfrak{g}(i)
, and
\mathfrak{n}_{0}=\mathfrak{n}=\bigoplus_{i\leqslant-1}\mathfrak{g}(i)
. For a Lagrangian
\ell
, we have
\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}=\mathfrak{n}_{\ell}
.

Let
U_{\ell},N_{\ell}\subset G
be subgroups of
G
with Lie algebras
\mathfrak{u}_{\ell},\mathfrak{n}_{\ell}
respectively;
U_{\ell}
naturally acts on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
and we can consider moment maps

\mu_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}\colon\mathfrak{g}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*},\,\mu_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}}\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*},\,\mu_{T^{*}\mathcal{B}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*}
.

Proposition 2.17
.

The coadjoint action maps

N_{\ell}\times S(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu^{-1}_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}(\chi),\,N_{\ell}\times\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}}(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}}(\chi),\,N_{\ell}\times\widetilde{S}(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{T^{*}\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(\chi)

are isomorphisms inducing identifications:

S(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}^{-1}(\chi)/N_{\ell},\,\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}}^{-1}(\chi)/N_{\ell},\,\widetilde{S}(\chi)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{T^{*}\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(\chi)/N_{\ell}.

Proof.

The same argument as in
[
GG02
, Lemma 2.1]
works, see also
[
Los12
]
.
∎

Remark 2.18
.

For a Lagrangian
\ell
, Proposition
2.17
realizes
S(\chi)
,
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)
as Hamiltonian reductions of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
by
U_{\ell}
at
\chi|_{\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}}\in\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*}
, i.e.,
S(\chi)=\mathfrak{g}/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}
,
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}/\!/\!/\!_{\chi}U_{\ell}
,
\widetilde{S}(\chi)=T^{*}\mathcal{B}/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}
.

2.4.3.
Parabolic Slodowy varieties

Let us now define
parabolic
Slodowy varieties. They will depend on a choice of parabolic
T\subset P\subset G
. Recall morphisms

\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
,
\widetilde{\pi}_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/W}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
.

We define

\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})=\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N}),\quad\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}):=\widetilde{\pi}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(S_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\chi)\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p})).

We will call
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
a
parabolic
Slodowy variety (called
S3
variety in
[
Web11
]
).

The following proposition is completely analogous to Proposition
2.17
above.
Consider the natural action of
U_{\ell}
on
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
,
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}
and the corresponding moment maps
\mu_{T^{*}\mathcal{P}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*},\,\mu_{T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}}\colon T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}^{*}
.

Proposition 2.19
.

The coadjoint action maps

N_{\ell}\times\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(\chi),\quad N_{\ell}\times\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu^{-1}_{T^{*}\mathcal{P}}(\chi)

are isomorphisms inducing identifications:

\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu_{T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(\chi)/N_{\ell},\quad\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mu^{-1}_{T^{*}\mathcal{P}}(\chi)/N_{\ell}.

Lemma 2.20
.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
such that
Z_{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)=\mathfrak{l}
, the natural morphism
\widetilde{S}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow S(\chi)\cap\mathbb{O}_{\lambda}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Recall that
\widetilde{S}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is the preimage of
S(\chi)
under the morphism
T^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. It follows from Lemma
2.12
that this morphism factors through the isomorphism
T^{*}_{\lambda}\mathcal{P}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,{\mathbb{O}}_{\lambda}
. The desired follows.
∎

Set
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p}):=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})]
. The natural morphism
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is a symplectic resolution. The contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
S(\chi)
induces the action on
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})=\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(S(\chi)\cap\mathcal{N})
which, in turn, induces the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
on
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
.

Lemma 2.21
.

The
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action as above contracts every connected component of
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
to the unique fixed point.

Proof.

Let us first of all note that the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action contracts
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
to
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
(use that
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\chi)
is proper and that we already know that
S(\chi)
is conical). It follows that the algebra
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}]
is non-negatively graded. It remains to check that
\operatorname{dim}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}]_{0}=|\operatorname{Comp}\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})|
. Pick a degree
0
function
f\colon\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Consider the proper variety
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
. Function
f
is constant on every connected component of
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
. Moreover,
f
has degree
0
, so it must be constant on the attractor (with respect to the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action) to every connected component of
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
. We conclude that the image of
f
is a finite set of points of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Every connected component of
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is irreducible, so its image under
f
is also irreducible, hence, a point.
∎

Remark 2.22
.

We note that
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
may have several connected components. Let us list two examples. The relation between them is explained in
Remark

5.3
.

1)

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(6)
, pick
\chi
corresponding to the partition
(3,3)
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a parabolic with Levi factor
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(1)^{2}\times\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(2)\subset\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(6)
. Then
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is a disjoint union of two Kleinian singularities of type
A_{1}
.

2)

Let
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(7)
, pick
\chi
corresponding to the partition
(3,1,1,1,1)
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a parabolic with Levi factor
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(3)\subset\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{o}(7)
. Then
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is a disjoint union of two Kleinian singularities of type
A_{1}
.

Let us now pick a pair of parabolic subalgebras
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathfrak{g}
sharing the same Borel
\mathfrak{b}
. containing the Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathfrak{q}
be the standard Levi subalgebra of
\mathfrak{q}
. Let
\chi_{\mathfrak{m}}\in\mathfrak{m}^{*}
be a regular nilpotent in
\mathfrak{m}^{*}
that lies in
\mathfrak{b}^{*}
. For future use, we introduce a new notation for the parabolic Slodowy variety to
\chi_{\mathfrak{m}}
:

\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})=\widetilde{S}(\chi_{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{p}).

2.5.
Quantizations of
X
and
Y

For a conical Poisson scheme
Z
a
graded quantization
of the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
is (see for example
[
Los17a
, Section 2.2]
):

•

a sheaf of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-algebras
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
in conical topology on
Z
equipped with a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action by algebra automorphisms such that
t\cdot\hbar=t^{2}\hbar
,

•

an isomorphism
\iota\colon\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}/(\hbar)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{O}_{Z}
of sheaves

such that:

•

\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
,

•

[\mathcal{D}_{\hbar},\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}]\subset\hbar\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
. This produces a Poisson bracket on
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}/(\hbar)
given by
\{a+\hbar\mathcal{D}_{\hbar},b+\hbar\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}\}=\frac{[a,b]}{\hbar}+\hbar\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
.

•

\iota
is a graded Poisson isomorphism.

We note that for affine scheme
Z
taking the global sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}
we get the definition of a graded quantization of
A=\mathbb{C}[Z]
. Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity, and assume that it admits a symplectic resolution
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}X
. In this section, we discuss graded quantizations of
\mathbb{C}[X]
and
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
.

The graded quantizations of
Y
are parametrized by the vector space
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
via the so called
period map

\operatorname{Per}
from the isomorphism classes of quantizations to
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
(see
[
BK04
]
,
[
Los12
, Section 2.3]
). We will denote the quantization that corresponds to
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
by
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}
.
Let
Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}
be the universal Poisson deformation of
Y
, see
Proposition

2.2
. We can talk about graded formal quantizations of
Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
that are now required to be sheaves of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X}][\hbar]
-algebras, where the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
is given by
t\cdot p=t^{-2}p
. It was shown in
[
BK04
, Section 6.2]
that
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}}
admits a canonical graded quantization to be denoted by
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
. It satisfies the following property: its specialization to
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
coincides with
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}
.

Warning 2.23
.

In this section, we talk about the graded quantizations, while all the provided references consider graded
formal
quantizations. To get a graded quantization from a graded formal quantization, we consider the subsheaf of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-finite elements. The inverse map is given by considering the completion along the ideal generated by
\hbar
.

We set
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}=\operatorname{\Gamma}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda})
. By the Grauert-Riemenschneider theorem,
R\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{Y})=\mathbb{C}[X]
. From here one deduces that the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}
is a graded quantization of
\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})=\mathbb{C}[X]
. Similarly, the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}=\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}})
is a graded quantization of
\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}]
over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X}]
.

In what follows, we often specialize graded quantizations to the parameter
\hbar=1
. To describe the resulting object, for a conical Poisson scheme
Z
, we can similarly define a
filtered quantzation
of the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
to be:

•

a sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}
-filtered algebras
\mathcal{D}
in conical topology on
Z
,

•

an isomorphism
\iota\colon\gr\mathcal{D}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{O}_{Z}
of sheaves

such that:

•

the filtration
\mathcal{D}_{\leq i}
on
\mathcal{D}
is complete and separated,

•

[\mathcal{D}_{\leq i},\mathcal{D}_{\leq j}]\subset\mathcal{D}_{\leq i+j-2}
. This produces a Poisson bracket on
\gr\mathcal{D}
given by

\{a+\mathcal{D}_{\leq i-1},b+\mathcal{D}_{\leq j-1}\}=[a,b]+\mathcal{D}_{\leq i+j-3}.

•

\iota
is a graded Poisson isomorphism.

For a conical Poisson algebra
A
, we recover the definition of a filtered quantization of
A
by taking the global sections of a filtered quantization of
\mathcal{O}_{\Spec(A)}
. The filtered quantizations
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda},\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda},\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
of our interest are obtained as the specializations of the graded quantizations
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda},\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}},\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda},\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
at
\hbar=1
.

Let us also mention that for a vector space
\mathfrak{a}
mapping linearly to
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
, we can consider the corresponding base changes
X_{\mathfrak{a}}
,
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}
and the corresponding quantizations
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}
,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}
. We will only deal with
\mathfrak{a}
such that the following assumption holds.

Assumption 2.24
.

There exists a nonempty open subset
U\subset\mathfrak{a}
such that the morphism
Y_{U}\rightarrow X_{U}
becomes an isomorphism.

Recall (see Proposition
2.4
above) that in
[
Nam15
, Main Theorem]
Namikawa proved that the set
\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
of
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
for which the map
Y_{\lambda}\rightarrow X_{\lambda}
fails to be an isomorphism is the union of certain hyperplanes. He also proves that the complement to these hyperplanes contains the ample cone of
Y
(again see Proposition
2.4
above, also compare with
[
Kal08
, Lemma 2.5]
). In particular, we see that Assumption
2.24
holds if the following assumption holds.

Assumption 2.25
.

The image of
\mathfrak{a}
in
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
contains the Chern class
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}
of an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(Y)
.

It follows from Lemma
2.20
that the Assumption
2.25
(hence, the Assumption
2.24
) is satisfied for the family
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})\rightarrow S_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
.

Lemma 2.26
.

The Assumption
2.25
holds for
X=\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
,
Y=S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
, and
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})=H^{2}(T^{*}\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})
mapping naturally to
\mathfrak{h}_{X}=H^{2}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}),\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is a closed subvariety of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
so every ample line bundle on
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
restricts to an ample line bundle on
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
. The claim follows.
∎

2.5.1.
Quantizations of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}

Fix a parabolic subgroup
T\subset B\subset P\subset G
and recall
\mathcal{P}=G/P
.
In this subsection, we describe quantizations of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}=\operatorname{Lie}P
, and let
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the unipotent radical,
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the Levi quotient. There is a natural isomorphism
H^{2}(T^{*}\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})\simeq H^{2}(\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, where
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})=(\mathfrak{l}/[\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}])^{*}
.
Note that the map
\mathfrak{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathfrak{l}/[\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}]
induces a natural embedding
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Recall that by construction
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
is the sheaf on
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}=G\times_{P}[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}]^{\perp}
. The cotangent bundle
T^{*}(G/[P,P])\simeq G\times^{[P,P]}[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}]^{\perp}
admits a tautological map
p
to
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}
given by
[(g,\xi)]\mapsto[(g,\xi)]
. Moreover,
p\colon T^{*}(G/[P,P])\rightarrow T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}
is a principal
P/[P,P]
-bundle. Let
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}(G/[P,P])
be the sheaf of homogeneous differential operators on
G/[P,P]
, and
\underline{\mathcal{D}}_{\hbar}(G/[P,P])
be the microlocalization of
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}(G/[P,P])
to a quantization of
T^{*}(G/[P,P])
(see, for example,
[
Gin86
]
for the details on the microlocalization). Now,

\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})=p_{*}(\underline{\mathcal{D}}_{\hbar}(G/[P,P]))^{P/[P,P]}.

Remark 2.27
.

For
P=B
, the definition above tells us that
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})
is the sheaf of
T
-invariants in
\underline{\mathcal{D}}_{\hbar}(T^{*}(G/U))
.

Algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
is by definition equal to global sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
. By the construction above,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})\simeq\Gamma(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{D}_{\hbar}(G/[P,P]))^{P/[P,P]}
. The map
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
