is the quantum comoment map for the
right
action of
P/[P,P]
on
G/[P,P]
.

Consider the quantum comoment map for the left action of
G
on
G/[P,P]
. It induces the homomorphism
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
from the homogenized universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
Z_{\hbar}\subset\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
be the center. Let
\HC_{\hbar}\colon Z_{\hbar}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}
be the homogeneous version of the Harish-Chandra isomorphism, where the action of
W
on
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
is given by:

(2.5.1)

w.s(\lambda,\hbar_{0})=s(w^{-1}(\lambda+\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})-\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{g}},\hbar_{0})

for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
,
\hbar_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
,
s\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
and
\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}
being the half sum of positive roots w.r.t. the fixed Borel
B
. Using the identification
HC_{\hbar}
, we obtain the homomorphism
Z_{\hbar}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
given by the composition
Z_{\hbar}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{G}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
, where the latter map is induced by the embedding
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

Lemma 2.28
.

(a)
The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Z_{\hbar}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})}

(b)
For
P=B
, the induced homomorphism
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Part
(a)
follows from
[
Los12
, proof of Lemma 5.2.1 (3)]
. Part
(b)
follows from
[
Los12
, Lemma 5.2.1 (4)]
.
∎

Setting
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})=\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
and using Lemma
2.28
, we obtain the homomorphism of graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebras:

\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}).

For
\hbar=0
, the homomorphism above identifies with the pull back homomorphism for the natural morphism
G\times^{P}[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}]^{\perp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}/{W}}\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
.

Remark 2.29
.

In type
A
, the homomorphism
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is surjective (see
[
Los12
, Lemma 5.2.1 (5)]
).
The homomorphism
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is not surjective in general, but it is surjective for Zariski generic
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
.
It will also be surjective after setting
\hbar=1
and restricting to
integral

\lambda
such that
\widetilde{\lambda}
is
\mathfrak{p}
-antidominant and
\mathfrak{l}
-regular
(see
[
WJ82
, Theorem 3.8]
).

Similar statements about surjectivity of
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
has appeared in
[
WJ82
, Corollary 3.9]
and
[
Los12
, Remark 5.2.2]
. Unfortunately, the results stated may not be precise. In particular, one should not expect
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is surjective for all integral
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. A counterexample is the following.

Consider
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{5}
and
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{gl}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{g}
. Then
\mathfrak{l}/[\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}]
is one-dimensional. Let
\lambda
be the linear function on
\mathfrak{l}
given by
\lambda(X)=\operatorname{tr}(X)
. This choice corresponds to the canonical quantization in
[
LMM21
, Section 5.1]
, in particular
\lambda=\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}-\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}
. Now, for a parabolic
P
that contains
L
, consider the map
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The image of
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the closure of
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, the subregular nilpotent orbit of
\mathfrak{so}_{5}
. On the other hand,
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}:=\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\mathbb{O})
is a double cover of
\mathbb{O}
. Let
\Pi
be
\Aut_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
. From
[
LMM21
, Corollary 6.5.6]
, the image of
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is
(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))^{\Pi}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
. The action of
\Pi
on
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
is lifted from the non-trivial action of
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[T^{*}\mathcal{P}])=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
, and
\gr(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))^{\Pi}\simeq\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{O}]).
In particular,
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is not surjective.

We have described the quantizations
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
. It is
not
true that the specialization of
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
at
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
has period
\lambda
. For that to be true, we have to
modify
the
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
as follows.
Let
\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
be the half sum of all
T
-weights of
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Recall that we can identify
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})^{*}=\mathfrak{l}/[\mathfrak{l},\mathfrak{l}]
with the center
\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{l})
.
Let
\iota\colon\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]=S^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{l}))[\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
be the quantum comoment map for the right action of
P/[P,P]
on
G/[P,P]
).
Consider the map
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
defined as the composition of
\iota
with the automorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
induced by the isomorphism
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}
given by
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto(\lambda-\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}},\hbar_{0})
.
From now on, we will consider the
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-module structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
induced by
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Remark 2.30
.

Note that
\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}}=\rho_{\mathfrak{g}}-\rho_{{\mathfrak{l}}}
, where
\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}
is the half sum of positive roots for
\mathfrak{l}
.

Warning 2.31
.

Note that we have shifted the
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-,
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-module structures on
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})
, so it is
not
true anymore that the homomorphism
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
-linear. Consider the homomorphism
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
induced by the embedding
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{*}\oplus\mathbb{C}
given by
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto(\lambda-\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}\hbar_{0},\hbar_{0})
.
After composing this homomorphism with
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the induced
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-action on
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
makes the homomorphism
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-linear.
In particular, for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, specialization of
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
at
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})
induces the homomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar_{0},\lambda-\hbar_{0}\rho_{{\mathfrak{l}}}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar_{0},\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
let
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
be the sheaf of
\lambda+\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-twisted differential operators on
\mathcal{P}
, and let
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
be its homogeneous version. We denote by
D_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
,
D_{\hbar,\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
the global sections of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
,
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
respectively.

Proposition 2.32
.

Fix
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
. Then the microlocalization
\underline{\mathcal{D}}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
(see
[
BPW16
, Section 4.1]
and references therein) of

\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
to
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
coincides with
\mathcal{D}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
and is the filtered quantization of
X=T^{*}\mathcal{P}

with period
\lambda
. In particular,
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})=D_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
.

Proof.

It follows from the definition that
\mathcal{D}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
is the microlocalization of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})
to
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
.
It follows from
[
BPW16
, Proposition 4.4]
that the period of
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})
equals
\lambda+\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}}-\frac{c_{1}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})}{2}
. Since
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
is isomorphic to the induced vector bundle
G\times^{P}\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}
, and
\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}=(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})^{*}\simeq\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}
(as
T
-modules), we have
c_{1}(T^{*}(\mathcal{P}))=2\rho_{\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
∎

Remark 2.33
.

Proposition
2.32
also follows from
[
Los12
, Theorem 5.4.1]
.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, let
\widetilde{\lambda}=\lambda-\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}
. We fix the notations

\mathcal{U}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}=\mathcal{U}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g})=D_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}),\quad\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,\widetilde{\lambda}}=\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,\widetilde{\lambda}}(\mathfrak{g})=D_{\hbar,\widetilde{\lambda}}(\mathcal{B}).

Remark 2.34
.

The algebra
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,\widetilde{\lambda}}
can be described as follows. Recall that
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
is the homogenized universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
, and
Z_{\hbar}\subset\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
is the center of
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Consider the
twisted
Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\widetilde{HC}_{\hbar}\colon Z_{\hbar}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]^{W}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
, where the action of
W
is
standard
.
For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}

we define a central character
\mu_{\widetilde{\lambda}}\colon Z_{\hbar}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
by
\mu_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(z)=\widetilde{HC}_{\hbar}(z)(\widetilde{\lambda})
.
Then
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,\widetilde{\lambda}}
is isomorphic to the central reduction of
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}
via
\mu_{\widetilde{\lambda}}
.
Note also that for any
w\in W
,
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,\lambda}=\mathcal{U}_{\hbar,w(\lambda)}
.

2.5.2.
Quantizations of parabolic Slodowy varieties

We fix a nilpotent element
e\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Consider the corresponding parabolic Slodowy variety
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
(see Section
2.4
).
Recall that
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
can be obtained as a Hamiltonian reduction of
T^{*}\mathcal{P}/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}
(see Proposition
2.17
and Remark
2.18
).
Recall also that
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})=T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}
is the base change of the universal deformation of
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
via the pullback map
p\colon\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})=H^{2}(T^{*}\mathcal{P},\mathbb{C})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}),\mathbb{C})
.

Remark 2.35
.

Note that in type
A
the map
p
is surjective (see
[
BO11
, Section 2]
). In general, we do not know when the morphism
p
is surjective.

We then consider quantum Hamiltonian reductions of the natural quantizations of
T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}
and
\mathbb{C}[T^{*}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}\mathcal{P}]
, see
[
Los12
, Section 3.3, 3.4]
for generalities on quantum Hamiltonian reduction. Define

\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell},\quad\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell},

\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell},\quad\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(e,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell},

\mathcal{W}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})=D_{\hbar,\lambda}(\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell},\quad\mathcal{W}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})=D_{\lambda}(\mathcal{P})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}.

Remark 2.36
.

It is straightforward to check (using that the assumptions of
[
Los12
, Lemma 3.1.1]
are satisfied in our situation) that we have the canonical identification:

\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))/(\hbar-1)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{D}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))

and similar identifications for
\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))
, and
\mathcal{W}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
.

The grading on the algebras above comes from the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
T^{*}\mathcal{P}
corresponding to the cocharacter
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
given by
t\mapsto(h(t),t)
(recall that
h\in\mathfrak{g}
is the Cartan element of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple for
e
, abusing the notation we denote by
h\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T
the cocharacter corresponding to
h\in\mathfrak{h}
).

The following proposition is standard.

Proposition 2.37
.

(a)
The sheaf
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))
quantizes
\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
over
\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
.

(b)
We have:

\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\Gamma(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}),\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))),

\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{W}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}),\quad\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{W}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}).

(c)
For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
, the specialization
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))
is the quantization of
\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
with period
p(\lambda)
.

Proof.

Part
(a)
is standard (see, for example,
[
Los12
, Section 3.3]
) and follows from the freeness of the action of
U_{\ell}
on the fiber of the moment map over
\chi
(see Proposition
2.19
above).
First equality in part
(b)
follows from the fact that the quantum Hamiltonian reduction functors intertwine the global section functors, compare with
[
Los19a
, Section 3.3.4]
.

The identifications
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{W}_{\hbar,\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
,
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p}))=\mathcal{W}_{\lambda}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
are also standard (see
[
Los12
, Section 3.3]
) and follow from the fact that the assumptions of Lemma 3.3.1 in loc. cit. are satisfied in our situation (see Proposition
2.19
above).

Part
(c)
follows from the general results on periods of quantum Hamiltonian reductions (see

[
Los12
, Section 5]
) together with Proposition
2.32
.
∎

Let us give an alternative description of the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi))
.
Consider the finite
W
-algebra:

\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)=\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})/\!/\!/_{\chi}U_{\ell}=(\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})/\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\{x-\chi(x)\,|\,x\in\mathfrak{u}_{\ell}\})^{U_{\ell}}.

It is well-known that the center of
\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)
identifies with
Z_{\hbar}
via the natural embedding
Z_{\hbar}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)
(see, for example, the footnote on page 35 of
[
Pre07
]
attributing it to Ginzburg). It then follows from the definitions (c.f.
[
Los12
, Section 5.1]
) together with Lemma
2.28
that the natural maps
\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi))
,
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi))
induce the isomorphism:

\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}^{*}}(\widetilde{S}(\chi)).

Warning 2.38
.

Let us emphasize that the grading on
\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)
is
not
induced by the standard grading on
\mathcal{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
, it is induced by the
Kazhdan
grading on
\mathfrak{g}
defined as follows. Recall the
\ad h
-eigenspace decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{i}\mathfrak{g}(i)
, then the degree of
x\in\mathfrak{g}(i)
is
i+2
(the degree of
\hbar
is still
2
).

Applying the quantum Hamiltonian reduction by
U_{\ell}
to the homomorphism

\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p})}(T^{*}\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p})}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}),

we obtain the homomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-algebras (c.f.
[
Los12
, Lemma 5.2.1 (3)]
):

\Phi_{\mathfrak{p},\chi}\colon\mathcal{W}_{\hbar}(\chi)\otimes_{Z_{\hbar}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]=\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi))\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\chi,\mathfrak{p})).

It follows from Remark
2.29
that the homomorphism
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p},\chi}
becomes surjective after setting
\hbar=1
and specializing at a regular integral antidominant
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{p})
.

2.6.
Categories
\mathcal{O}
and localizations

Recall that
T_{X}
is a torus acting on
Y

in a Hamiltonian way. From now on, we assume that the set
Y^{T_{X}}
is finite.
Let us fix a co-character
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
.
Recall that a co-character
\nu
is
regular

if the set
Y^{\nu}
is finite (in other words, coincides with
Y^{T_{X}}
).
We obtain the decomposition of the vector space
\mathfrak{t}_{X,\mathbb{R}}:=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\times},T_{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
into the union of
open chambers
separated by the walls corresponding to non-regular co-characters
\nu
(see Section
2.1.4
).

2.6.1.
Categories
\mathcal{O}

We fix a parameter
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
and a regular co-character
\nu\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\times},T_{X})
. Consider the corresponding filtered quantization
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
. Recall that
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}=\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
. The action of
T_{X}\curvearrowright\mathcal{O}_{Y}
lifts canonically to the action
T_{X}\curvearrowright\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
and hence to the action
T_{X}\curvearrowright\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
.

The co-character
\nu
induces a grading
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,i}
on
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
.
Let

\mathcal{A}^{>0}_{\lambda}:=\bigoplus_{i>0}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,i}.

It follows from
[
BPW16
, Proposition 3.11]
that the action of
\nu

on
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
is Hamiltonian, so we have a comoment map
\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
. Let
h\in\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
be the image of
1\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-
\operatorname{mod}
be the category of finitely generated
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-modules and let
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\subset\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-
\operatorname{mod}
be the full subcategory, consisting of all modules where the action of
\mathcal{A}^{>0}_{\lambda}
is locally nilpotent and the action of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
is locally finite.

Let
\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
be the category of all coherent
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}
-modules and
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
be the full subcategory of modules that come with a good filtration stable under
h
and are supported on the contracting locus

Y_{+}
of
\nu
, i.e.,
Y_{+}:=\{x\in Y\,|\,\operatorname{lim}_{t\rightarrow 0}\nu(t)\cdot x~\operatorname{exists}\}
.
The set

\operatorname{Irr}(\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}))
of irreducible objects
can be naturally identified with
Y^{T_{X}}
(see
[
BLPW16
, Proposition 5.17]
and Section
2.7.3
below).

2.6.2.
Localizations

We have the global section functor
\Gamma\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-
\operatorname{mod}
.
We denote by
\operatorname{Loc}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
-
\operatorname{mod}\rightarrow\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
the left adjoint functor given by
N\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}N
(see
[
BPW16
, Section 4.2]
).
For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
, we say that abelian localization holds for
(\lambda,Y)
when the functors

\Gamma
and
\operatorname{Loc}
are quasi-inverse equivalences. Let
\eta\in H^{2}(Y,\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{h}_{X}
be the Chern class of an ample line bundle on
Y
. It is known (see
[
BPW16
, Corollary B.1]
) that for a sufficiently large integer
M
(depending on
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
) the abelian localization holds for
\lambda+m\chi
for all integers
m\geq M
.
In particular, the irreducible objects of

\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda+m\chi})
are parametrized by
Y^{T_{X}}
.

2.7.
Cartan subquotients (
B
-algebras)

The main references to this section are
[
Los17
, Sections 4,5]
and
[
BLPW16
, Section 5.1]
. For any
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring
\mathcal{A}
, set

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}):=\mathcal{A}_{0}/\sum_{i>0}\mathcal{A}_{-i}\mathcal{A}_{i},

ring
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
will be called the
Cartan subquotient
or a
B
-algebra
of
\mathcal{A}
.
We will apply this construction to the quantizations (polynomial, and filtered) of
\mathbb{C}[X]
,
\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}]
and more generally
\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}]
for some vector space
\mathfrak{a}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}
with
\mathbb{Z}
-grading induced by a (regular) cocharacter
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
as above. If
\mathcal{A}
is one of these quantizations, then the corresponding Cartan subquotient will be denoted by
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A})
. We will also use the notation
\mathcal{A}^{\geqslant 0}:=\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\mathcal{A}_{i}
. Note that there exists the natural surjection
\mathcal{A}^{\geqslant 0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
induced by the surjection
\mathcal{A}^{\geqslant 0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{0}
.

2.7.1.
Cartan subquotient as schematic fixed points

Let
A
be a graded commutative algebra, and let
X:=\operatorname{Spec}A
. The
\mathbb{Z}
-grading on
A
defines the action
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\curvearrowright X
. Let
X^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
be the
functor
:

X^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\colon{\bf{Schemes}}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow{\bf{Sets}}

from the category
{\bf{Schemes}}_{\mathbb{C}}
of schemes over
\mathbb{C}
to the category of
Sets
defined as follows (see
[
Fog73
]
,
[
Dri13
, Section 1.2]
):

X^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(S):=\operatorname{Maps}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(S,Y),~S\in{\bf{Schemes}}_{\mathbb{C}},

where the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
on
S
is trivial and
\operatorname{Maps}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(S,Y)
is the set of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant morphisms from
S
to
Y
.

The following proposition is well-known (see, for example,
[
KS
, Proposition 1.4]
).

Proposition 2.39
.

Functor
X^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is represented by an affine scheme whose ring of functions is exactly
\mathcal{C}(A)
.

Note that the functor
X^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is well-defined even if
X
is not affine, one can similarly define the functor
X^{T}
for an arbitrary variety with an action of some torus
T
. The following proposition holds by
[
Ive72
]
.

Proposition 2.40
.

Let
X
be a smooth algebraic
T
-variety. Then
X^{T}
is smooth.

2.7.2.
Sheaf version of Cartan subquotient

In this section, let
T=T_{X}
, and let
Y
be a symplectic resolution of
X
. Let
X_{\mathfrak{a}}
be a deformation of
X
over some vector space
\mathfrak{a}
mapping linearly to
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
. Let
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}
be the corresponding deformation of
Y
. Recall that we assume that the natural morphism
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}\rightarrow X_{\mathfrak{a}}
becomes an isomorphism after the restriction to some nonempty open subset
U\subset\mathfrak{a}
(see Assumption
2.24
above).

Following
[
Los17
, Section 5.2]
one can talk about the Cartan subquotients of
sheaves

\mathcal{D}
on
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}
(see
[
Los17
, Proposition 5.2]
), the resulting object will be a
sheaf
of algebras on the
T
-fixed points of
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}
(recall that
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}
is smooth by Proposition
2.40
above).

Lemma 2.41
.

There exists the unique isomorphism
\alpha:Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}\simeq Y^{T}\times\mathfrak{a}
of varieties over
\mathfrak{a}
, such that the restriction of
\alpha
to the fibers over
0\in\mathfrak{a}
is the identity map.

Proof.

It follows from
[
Nam10
, Section 1.2 and references therein]
that there exists a
T
-equivariant identification of
C^{\infty}
-manifolds over
\mathfrak{a}
that is equal to identity being restricted to the fibers over
0
:

(2.7.1)

Y_{\mathfrak{a}}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,Y\times\mathfrak{a}

(the action of
T
on the right-hand side is induced by the action of
T
on
Y
).

Passing to
T
-fixed points, we obtain the identification
\alpha:Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,Y^{T}\times\mathfrak{a}
of
C^{\infty}
-manifolds over
\mathfrak{a}
. The fact that the restriction of (
2.7.1
) to the fibers over
0
is equal to identity implies that the restriction of
\alpha
to the fibers of
0
is identity.

It remains to check that this identification is a morphism of algebraic varieties (then the fact that it is an isomorphism follows from Zariski’s main theorem).

Indeed, note that for an arbitrary smooth variety
Z
over
\mathbb{C}
its connected components considered as
C^{\infty}
-variety coincide with its connected components considered as an algebraic variety (let
Z_{i}
be a connected component of the algebraic variety
Z
, then
Z_{i}
is connected as an analytic manifold by
[
Sha13
, Chapter VII, § 2.2, Theorem 1]
, so it is also path-connected and this implies that
Z_{i}
is connected as a
C^{\infty}
-manifold). In particular, we see that
Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}
has
|Y^{T}|
connected components. Let
F
be one of them. The identification (
2.7.1
) induces the isomorphism
F\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\{p\}\times\mathfrak{a}
for some
p\in Y^{T}
. It remains to check that the identification above is algebraic. This is clear since the composition
F\hookrightarrow Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{a}}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\{p\}\times\mathfrak{a}
is algebraic.

∎

Lemma 2.42
.

There are canonical isomorphisms of sheaves of algebras

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}}[\hbar],\quad\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{Y^{T}}[\hbar],\quad\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{Y^{T}}.

Proof.

Since fixed
T
-points are isolated in
Y
by our assumption, lemma follows from
[
BLPW16
, Lemma 5.2]
.
∎

Using Lemmas
2.41
,
2.42
we obtain the natural homomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar]
-algebras

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})\rightarrow\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}))=\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar]

that at
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}
specializes to the natural homomorphism

(2.7.2)

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\lambda})\rightarrow\Gamma(Y^{T},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\lambda}))=\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hbar].

Remark 2.43
.

It follows from
[
BPW16
, Proposition 5.3]
(see also
[
Los17
, Proposition 5.3]
) that the composition (
2.7.2
) becomes
\mathbb{C}[\hbar]
-linear isomorphism for Zariski generic values of
\lambda
.

In what follows we are mostly interested in the case when
Y
has finitely many
T
-fixed points. To track that, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 2.44
.

The set
Y^{T}
is finite if and only if
X^{T}
consists of one point.

Proof.

If
X^{T}
is infinite, then
Y^{T}
is also infinite because the map
\pi
is proper and surjective. Assume now that
X^{T}
is finite. Since
T
commutes with the contracting action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
on
X
,
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
acts on
X^{T}
contracting it to the unique
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-fixed point
e
. This implies that
X^{T}=\{e\}
. It remains to check that
Y^{T}
is also finite. Consider the deformation
\pi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\colon Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\rightarrow X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
over
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
. It follows from the graded Nakayama lemma that the algebra
\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{T}]
is finitely generated over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X}]
. Morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
is an isomorphism over generic
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
so
\mathbb{C}[Y_{\lambda}^{T}]
is a finite-dimensional vector space for generic
\lambda
, hence, the set
Y_{\lambda}^{T}
is finite. It remains to recall that the spaces
Y_{\lambda}
,
Y
are diffeomorphic as
C^{\infty}
-manifolds with a
T
-action (cf.
[
Nam10
, Section 1, Step 2 and references therein]
, so we must have
Y^{T}=Y_{\lambda}^{T}
is finite.
∎

2.7.3.
Cartan subquotients and irreducible objects in category
\mathcal{O}

Let us now recall the relation between
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
and irreducible objects in the category
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
. Pick
\lambda
such that the abelian localization holds and the morphism (
2.7.2
) specialized at
\hbar=1
is an isomorphism:

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\Gamma(Y^{T},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}))=\bigoplus_{p\in Y^{T}}\mathbb{C}.

We see that the algebra
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
is finite-dimensional semisimple and has
|Y^{T}|
irreducible (one dimensional) representations labeled by the fixed points of
Y
. Let
\mathbb{C}_{p}
be the irreducible representation of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
corresponding to the point
p\in Y^{T}
. We can then form the
standard
module (see
[
BLPW16
, Section 5.2]
)

\Delta_{\lambda}(p)=\Delta(p)=\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\geqslant 0}}\mathbb{C}_{p}\in\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}),

where the action of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\geqslant 0}\curvearrowright\mathbb{C}_{p}
is induced by the natural surjection
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\geqslant 0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
.

Remark 2.45
.

Similarly, one can define the
costandard
module
\nabla(p)=\nabla_{\lambda}(p)
as follows (see
[
BLPW16
, Section 5.2]
):
\nabla(p)=(\mathbb{C}_{p}^{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{A})^{\star}
, here
\star
denotes the restricted duality.

By
[
BLPW16
, Proposition 5.17]
, the category
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
is highest weight, and therefore every
\Delta(p)
has
unique
irreducible quotient denoted by
L(p)
. Moreover, modules
\{L(p)\,|\,p\in Y^{T}\}
are pairwise non-isomorphic and form the complete list of simples in
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
. Recall that we have the equivalence of categories
\operatorname{Loc}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
. Applying
\operatorname{Loc}
to
L(p)
, we obtain irreducible objects in
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
. In this way we obtain the labeling
Y^{T}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\operatorname{Irr}(\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}))
. This is exactly the labeling mentioned in Section
2.6.2
.

Let us finally mention that every object
V\in\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
is naturally graded via the action by the commutator with
h
(recall that
h
is the image of
1
under the comoment map induced by
\nu
). It follows from the definitions that
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
acts on each graded component. Now, the
highest weight
component of
L(p)
is one-dimensional and the action of
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
on the corresponding one-dimensional space factors through the quotient
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
and is precisely the character of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
, corresponding to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
. In other words, we can read off the character
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
from the irreducible representation
L(p)
as the “highest weight” of
L(p)
considered as
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
-module. We note that we have a surjective specialization map
\mathcal{A}_{h,\mathfrak{a},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda,0}=\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,0}
, and therefore each element
x\in\mathcal{A}_{h,\mathfrak{a},0}
acts on
L(p)\in\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})
by a scalar.

Remark 2.46
.

Note that highest weights of
\Delta(p)
,
\nabla(p)
,
L(p)
are the same.

2.7.4.
From algebraic to sheaf Cartan subquotients

Recall that the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
-graded with
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathbb{C}\hbar
sitting in degree
2
. We denote the
i
-th graded piece by
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}^{i}
. Pick
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}
and
\hbar_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, we can consider the specialization
\mathcal{A}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda)}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}
to the point
(\hbar_{0},\lambda)\in\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathfrak{a}
. We see that for
\hbar_{0}\neq 0
the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(corresponding to the
\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
-grading above) identifies
\mathcal{A}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda)}
and
\mathcal{A}_{(1,\hbar_{0}^{-1}\lambda)}=\mathcal{A}_{\hbar_{0}^{-1}\lambda}
. Consider the composition

(2.7.3)

\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a},0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})\rightarrow\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}))=\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar].

Pick
x\in\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a},0}^{i}
for an even
i
.

Proposition 2.47
.

(a) For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{a}
such that the abelian localization holds and
\hbar_{0}\neq 0
, the image of
x_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda)}\in\mathcal{A}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda),0}
under

\mathcal{A}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda),0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda)})\rightarrow\Gamma(Y^{T},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{(\hbar_{0},\lambda)}))=\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]

is equal to the collection of scalars by which
\hbar_{0}^{{{i/2}}}x
acts on the highest components of the irreducible modules in
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar_{0}^{-2}\lambda})
.

(b) For
\hbar_{0}=0
the composition (
2.7.3
) identifies with the pull back homomorphism

\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}]_{0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[Y_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}]=\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a}].

(c) If
\mathfrak{a}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}
is such that the Assumption
2.25

holds, then
(a)
determines the image of
x
uniquely.

Proof.

Since the homomorphism (
2.7.3
) is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant, it is enough to prove
(a)
for
\hbar_{0}=1
. For
\hbar_{0}=1
the claim follows from the definitions. Part
(b)
is clear. To show (c), we note that any polynomial
f(t)
is uniquely determined by its values at infinitely many points. Thus,
(c)
follows from
[
BPW16
, Corollary B.1]
by restricting to lines
\ell(x)|_{x\in\mathfrak{a}}
of the form
\ell(x)=\{x+c\tilde{\chi}\,|\,c\in\mathbb{C}\}
, where
\tilde{\chi}\in\mathfrak{a}
is a preimage of the first Chern class
\chi
of an ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}
(see the notations of Assumption
2.25
).
∎

Note that we are not claiming in Proposition
2.47
(b) that
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})/(\hbar)
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}[X^{T}_{\mathfrak{a}}]
. Although, it is clearly true that we have the identification
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a},0}/(\hbar)=\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}]_{0}
.

We always have a surjective homomorphism
\mathbb{C}[X^{T}_{\mathfrak{a}}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})/(\hbar)
. Moreover, in the following proposition
we explain when this map is indeed an isomorphism.

Proposition 2.48
.

Assume that
\operatorname{dim}\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]\leqslant|Y^{T}|
. Then:

(a) We have
\operatorname{dim}\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]=|Y^{T}|
.

(b) Cartan subquotient
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})
is a flat (hence, free) module over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar]
of rank
|Y^{T}|
.

(c) The natural (surjective) morphism
\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}^{T}]=\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{a}}])\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})/(\hbar)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It is straightforward to check that we have the surjective homomorphism
\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})/(\hbar,\mathfrak{a}^{*})
. Using the inequality
\operatorname{dim}\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]\leqslant|Y^{T}|
together with the graded Nakayama lemma, we see that it is enough to check that the fibers of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})
over a generic point of
\mathfrak{a}\oplus\mathbb{C}
have dimension equal to
|Y^{T}|
.

Recall that we have the homomorphism
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[Y^{T}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar]
. The same argument as the one in
[
Los17
, Proposition 5.3]
shows that this map becomes an isomorphism for Zariski generic
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\in\mathfrak{a}\oplus\mathbb{C}
. This proves the claim.
∎

3.
Equivariant cohomology

3.1.
Basic results

Consider a torus
T
acting on a variety
X
(not necessarily with finitely many fixed points). This section consists of several results on equivariant cohomology
H_{T}^{*}(X)
that we often use in the paper. Our main reference will be
[
AF23
]
.

By the definition,
H_{T}^{*}(X)
is the cohomology ring
H^{*}(\mathbb{E}\times^{T}X)
where
\mathbb{E}
is a certain universal principal
T
-bundle of
X
(see
[
AF23
]
). After the identification
T\simeq(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{r}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\simeq(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\})^{r}
). Consider a
T
-equivariant vector bundle
E
over
X
, then
\mathbb{E}\times^{T}E
is a vector bundle over
\mathbb{E}\times^{T}X
, and the equivariant Chern classes
c_{i}^{T}(E)
are defined as the Chern classes of
\mathbb{E}\times^{T}E
.

When
X
is a point, we have
H_{T}^{*}(\pt)
is
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
, the algebra of functions on the Lie algebra of
T
. The identification is constructed as follows. To every character
\lambda\colon T\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
we can associate the one dimensional vector bundle
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}
over
X=\operatorname{pt}
. Then the identification
H^{*}_{T}(\operatorname{pt})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
is given by
c_{1}^{T}(\mathbb{C}_{\lambda})\mapsto-\lambda
. More generally, every
T
-equivariant bundle over
X=\operatorname{pt}
is simply a
T
-module
E
. Then,
c_{i}^{T}(E)\mapsto e_{i}(-\chi_{1},\ldots,-\chi_{\operatorname{rk}E})
, where
\chi_{1},...,\chi_{\rk E}
is the list of characters of
E
.

In this paper, we will often consider cases with the following features.

(1)

T
acts on
X
with finitely many fixed points.

(2)

H^{*}_{T}(X)
is a free module over
H^{*}_{T}(\pt)
.

With these conditions, the localization theorem in equivariant cohomology (see, e.g.,
[
AF23
, Section 5]
) implies that the pullback map
\iota^{*}:H_{T}^{*}(X)\rightarrow H_{T}^{*}(X^{T})
is an injection. Moreover, it becomes an isomorphism after localizing at certain multiplicative subset
S\subset\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
that is generated by homogeneous polynomials. When
T\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we have
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]=\mathbb{C}[t]
, the polynomial ring in one variable. In this case, we can always choose
S
as the set generated by
t
.

3.1.1.
Specialization and pullback

Consider a torus
T
that acts on a smooth variety
X
. Let
\mathfrak{t}
be the Lie algebra of
T
, and
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}
. The equivariant cohomology
H^{*}_{T}(X)
is an algebra over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
. We keep the assumption that
H^{*}_{T}(X)
is a free module over
H^{*}_{T}(\pt)
, this will always be the case in our paper.

Write
H^{*}_{T}(X)_{\lambda}
for the specialization of
H^{*}_{T}(X)
at
\lambda
. Torus
T
acts on
X
, so we can regard
\lambda
as a vector field on
X
to be denoted
\lambda_{X}
. Write
X^{\lambda_{X}}\subset X
for the subvariety of zeroes of this vector field. By the Berline-Vergne localization theorem (
[
BV85a
, Proposition 2.1]
,
[
AB84
, Sections 4,5]
), we have the following isomorphism

(3.1.1)

H^{*}_{T}(X)_{\lambda}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,H^{*}(X^{\lambda})

induced by the natural map
H^{*}_{T}(X)\rightarrow H^{*}(X^{\lambda})
. In general, it makes sense to consider the pullback map in equivariant cohomology. As a consequence, we have a natural map
h_{X}\colon H_{T}^{*}(\pt)

\rightarrow H_{T}^{*}(X)
, and
H_{T}^{*}(X)
is a module over
H_{T}^{*}(\pt)=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
.
Note that the isomorphism (
3.1.1
) commutes with the pullback map as follows. Consider a
T
-equivariant morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
. Then the isomorphism above identifies
f_{\lambda}^{*}:H^{*}_{T}(Y)_{\lambda}\rightarrow H^{*}_{T}(X)_{\lambda}
with the pullback for the fixed-point varieties
H^{*}(Y^{\lambda})\rightarrow H^{*}(X^{\lambda})
.

A consequence is the following. Consider a
T
-equivariant morphism
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
. Assume that both
X
and
Y
are such that
H_{T}^{*}(X)
,
H_{T}^{*}(Y)
are free over
H^{*}_{T}(\operatorname{pt})
. Then
f^{*}\colon H^{*}_{T}(Y)\rightarrow H^{*}_{T}(X)
is surjective if and only if
f^{*}\colon H^{*}(Y)\rightarrow H^{*}(X)
is surjective by graded Nakayama lemma.

3.2.
Equivariant cohomology of partial flag varieties

In this section and the next, we start with general results on flag and partial flag varieties. We then explain explicit results for the cases
G
of classical type.

Let
G
be a simple simply-connected Lie group of rank
n
. Let
T_{G}
be a maximal torus of
G
and let
\mathfrak{h}_{G}
be its Lie algebra. Let
B\subset P\subset G
be a Borel and a parabolic subgroup of
G
that contains
T_{G}
. Let
M
be the Levi subgroup of
P
. Write
W_{G}
and
W_{M}
for the Weyl groups of
G
and
M
. Write
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{P}
for the flag variety
G/B
and the partial flag variety
G/P
, respectively. The torus
T_{G}
naturally acts on
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{P}
by multiplication from the left.

Proposition 3.1
.

[
Kaj10
, Section 5]

(1)

There is a canonical identification
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{B})\simeq\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}_{G}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}_{G}]^{W_{G}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}_{G}]
. In particular, the equivariant cohomology
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{B})
is a free module over
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\pt)=\mathbb{C}[t_{1},...,t_{n}]
of rank
|W_{G}|
.

(2)

The natural pullback
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{B})
identifies
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{P})
with the
W_{M}
-invariant subring of
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{B})
. In other words,
H^{*}_{T_{G}}(\mathcal{P})\simeq\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{G}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}
.

A simple corollary is the following.

Corollary 3.2
.

Consider a torus
T\subset T_{G}
and its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
. The equivariant cohomology ring
H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{P})
is the quotient
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{G}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}
of the polynomial algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}=H^{*}_{T}(\pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}H^{*}_{M}(\pt)
.

3.2.1.

Let us describe the identification

(3.2.1)

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{G}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{P})

from Corollary
3.2
.

For every finite dimensional representation
V
of
M
, let
\mathcal{V}
be the induced vector bundle
G\times^{P}V
on
\mathcal{P}
(the action of
P
on
V
is via the natural surjection
P\twoheadrightarrow M
).
Let
\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V^{*}
be the character of
V^{*}
considered as an element of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}
. Similarly, to every finite dimensional representation
S
of
T
, we can associate the trivial vector bundle
\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{P}\times S
with the
T
-equivariant structure induced by the action of
T
on
S
. The character
\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}S^{*}
can be considered as an element of
\mathfrak{h}^{*}\subset\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]
. The isomorphism (
3.2.1
) is uniquely determined as follows:

[\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}S^{*}\otimes\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V^{*}]\mapsto c_{1}^{T}(\mathcal{S})c^{T}_{1}(\mathcal{V})~\text{for every}~S\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathrm{f.d.}}T,~V\in\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathrm{f.d.}}M.

Note that
c_{i}^{T}(\mathcal{S})\in H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{P})
is the image of the functional
x\mapsto e_{i}(\alpha_{1}(x),\ldots,\alpha_{s}(x))
(considered as an element of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]\otimes 1
), where
\alpha_{i}
are eigenvalues of
T
acting on
S^{*}
(counted with multiplicities), we will denote this functional by
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}S^{*})\otimes 1
. Similarly,
c_{i}^{T}(\mathcal{V})
is the image of the functional
x\mapsto e_{i}(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{v})
(considered as an element of
1\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}
), where
\alpha_{i}
are eigenvalues of
T_{G}
acting on
V^{*}
(counted with multiplicities), this functional will be denoted by
1\otimes e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V^{*})
.

Let us describe another possible way to determine the isomorphism (
3.2.1
). Pick any
collection
S_{k}
,
V_{l}
of representations of
T
,
M
such that
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}S_{k}^{*})
,
e_{j}(\operatorname{ch}V_{l}^{*})
generate
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]
and
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{M}}
respectively.
Then (
3.2.1
) is uniquely determined as follows:

[e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}S_{k}^{*})\otimes e_{j}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V_{l}^{*})]\mapsto c_{i}^{T}(\mathcal{S}_{k})c_{j}^{T}(\mathcal{V}_{l}).

Explicitly, the kernel of the map

(3.2.2)

e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}S_{k}^{*})\otimes e_{j}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V_{l}^{*})\mapsto c_{i}^{T}(\mathcal{S}_{k})c_{j}^{T}(\mathcal{V}_{l})

is generated by
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}V^{*})\otimes 1-1\otimes e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V^{*})
, where
V
runs through finite dimensional representations of
G
. Let’s see that the difference as above maps to zero via the map (
3.2.2
).
Indeed, for every
G
-module
V
, the corresponding induced vector bundle
\mathcal{V}=G\times^{P}V
is trivial, the isomorphism
G\times^{P}V\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{P}\times V
is given by
[(g,v)]\mapsto([g],gv)
. This isomorphism is
T
-equivariant w.r.t. the diagonal action of
T
on
\mathcal{P}\times V
. It follows that the elements
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}V^{*})\otimes 1
,
1\otimes e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V^{*})
both map to
c_{i}^{T}(\mathcal{V})
.

Alternatively, the kernel is generated by
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}}V_{p}^{*})\otimes 1-1\otimes e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V_{p}^{*})
, where
\{V_{p}\}
is any collection of representations of
G
such that
e_{i}(\operatorname{ch}_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}V_{p})
generate
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{G}}
.

3.3.
Fixed points of partial flag varieties

Let
T\subset G
be a torus. The following is a general result for the
T
-fixed point varieties of partial flags. Fix a parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
. Let
\mathcal{P}^{T}
be the corresponding fixed point variety. Write
W_{L}
for the Weyl group of
P
. Write
M
for the connected component of
C_{G}(T)
that contains
\Id
. Let
W_{M}
and
W_{G}
be the Weyl group of
M
and
G
, respectively.

Proposition 3.3
.

[
Ste76
, Proposition 3.3]

The variety
\mathcal{P}^{T}
parametrizes the parabolic subalgebras
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{g}
that are conjugated to
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{l}
nonempty. It has
|W_{L}\setminus W_{G}/W_{M}|
connected components. For
[w]\in W_{L}\setminus W_{G}/W_{M}
, the corresponding component is isomorphic to
M/(M\cap(w.P))
, a partial flag variety of
M
, via the map
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\mapsto\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{l}
.

Remark 3.4
.

The intersection
(M\cap(w.P))
depends on the choice of
w
, but for all
w
in the same double
W_{M}-W_{L}
coset, the corresponding varieties
M/(M\cap(w.P))
are isomorphic.

Let
M
be a Levi subgroup of
G
. Consider
T=Z_{M}(G)
and
P=B
. The next corollary describes the variety
\mathcal{B}^{T}
.

Corollary 3.5
.

(a)
Each connected component
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}
of
\mathcal{B}^{T}
is then isomorphic to the flag variety
\mathcal{B}_{M}
of
\mathfrak{m}
via the map
\mathfrak{b}\mapsto\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{m}
.

(b)

\mathfrak{b}_{1},\mathfrak{b}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}^{T}
lie in the same connected component iff they are conjugated by
M
.

(c)
Group
W
acts transitively on the set of connected components of
\mathcal{B}^{T}
, realizing this set as
W/W_{M}
.

Consider
H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{B}^{T})
as a quotient of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]
, then it makes sense to talk about the set-theoretic support of
\Spec(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{B}^{T}))
as a subset of
\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}_{G}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

The set-theoretic support of
\Spec(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{B}^{T}))
consists of
|W_{G}|/|W_{M}|
irreducible components, each isomorphic to
\mathfrak{h}
.

Proof.

From
Corollary

3.5
, we know that each component of
\mathcal{B}^{T}
is a flag variety that is fixed by
T
, so the set-theoretic support of each component is isomorphic
\mathfrak{h}
by the description in
Proposition

3.1
. To show that two distinct components have distinct supports, we specialize to a generic element
\lambda
in
\mathfrak{h}
. We have
\mathcal{B}^{T}=\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}
in the notation of Section 3.1.1.

We choose a maximal Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}_{G}
of
G
so that
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{G}
. View
\lambda
as a point of
\mathfrak{h}_{G}
. Let
I_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}(\lambda)
be the maximal ideal that corresponds to
\lambda
in
\mathfrak{h}_{G}
. Using the isomorphism (
3.2.1
),
H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{B})_{\lambda}
can be rewritten as
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]^{W_{G}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]/I_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}(\lambda)^{W_{G}}=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]/I_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}(\lambda)^{W_{G}}
. The set-theoretic support of
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{G}]/I_{\mathfrak{h}_{G}}(\lambda)^{W_{G}})
as a subscheme of
\mathfrak{h}_{G}
is precisely the orbit
W_{G}.\lambda\subset\mathfrak{h}_{G}
. Now the variety
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}
has precisely
|W_{G}.\lambda|
connected components. Therefore, each component is supported on a single point of the orbit
W_{G}.\lambda
.
∎

3.4.
Explicit descriptions for
G
of types
ABC

We discuss the cases
G
of classical type and make explicit some results from the previous section. Assume that
G=\operatorname{SL}_{n}
and
B
is the subgroup of upperdiagonal matrices. Let
V
be the standard representation of
G
. Then, there exists the natural identification
\mathcal{B}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{F}
is the variety of complete flags in
V
. Let
T
be a torus acting linearly on
V
. For a point of
\mathcal{F}
, we use the notation
F^{\bullet}=F^{1}\subset\ldots\subset F^{n}
where
\dim F^{i}=i
. On
\mathcal{F}
, we have the tautological line bundles
\mathcal{F}^{i}
that parameterize the pair
(F^{\bullet},F^{i}/F^{i-1})
. It is easy to see that
\mathcal{F}^{i}=G\times^{B}\mathbb{C}_{i}
, where
\mathbb{C}_{i}
is the one-dimensional representation of
B\twoheadrightarrow T
, corresponding to the character
T\ni\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\mapsto t_{i}^{-1}
. Write
x_{i}
for
c_{1}^{T}(\mathcal{F}^{i})
.

The following proposition is a particular case of our discussion in Section
3.2.1
(applied to
G=\operatorname{SL}_{n}
,
p=1
and
V_{1}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
).

Proposition 3.7
.

[
AF23
, Proposition 4.4.1]

We have

H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F})=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}][x_{1},...,x_{n}]/(e_{i}(x)-c^{T}_{i}(V))_{i=1,...,n}

in which
e_{i}
is the elementary symmetric polynomial of
n
variables
x_{1},...,x_{n}
.

From this description, it makes sense to consider
\operatorname{Spec}(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}))
as a subscheme of
\mathfrak{h}\\
\times\mathbb{C}^{n}
. The following corollary is about a simple case of the set-theoretic support of
\operatorname{Spec}(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}))
.

Corollary 3.8
.

Assume that
T\simeq(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{k}
acts on
V
with constant weight
w
(so
T
fixes
\mathcal{F}
). Then the set-theoretic support of
\operatorname{Spec}(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}))
is the image of the embedding

\mathfrak{t}\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{n},\quad\lambda\mapsto(\lambda,w(\lambda),\ldots,w(\lambda))

where
w(\lambda)
is considered as a complex number.

In particular, the specialization of
\operatorname{Spec}(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}))
to any point
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}
is set-theoretically supported on a single point.

We have similar results for orthogonal and symplectic flag varieties. Consider a symplectic (resp. orthogonal) vector space
V
of dimensions
m=2n
(resp.
2n+1
). We assume that the action of
T
preserves the corresponding bilinear form. Then
T
acts on
\mathcal{F}
, the variety of maximal isotropic flags of
V
.

A maximal isotropic flag of
V
is again given by
F^{\bullet}=F^{1}\subset\ldots\subset F^{n}
where
\dim F^{i}=i
. Similarly, we have the line bundles
\mathcal{F}^{i}
and the first Chern classes
x_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,m
.
The following proposition is a particular case of our discussion in Section
3.2.1
. Namely, for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
or
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}
, we take
p=1
and
V_{1}=V
.
Using the fact that

e_{2i}(\operatorname{ch}V)=e_{2i}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},-x_{1},\ldots,-x_{n}))=e_{i}(-x_{1}^{2},\ldots,-x_{n}^{2})

are the generators of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]^{W_{G}}

we obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 3.9
.

[
AF23
, Proposition 13.3.1]

For
\mathfrak{g}
being equal to
\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
or
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+1}
we have

H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F})=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}][x_{1},...,x_{n}]/(e_{i}(-x_{1}^{2},...,-x_{n}^{2})-c_{2i}(V))_{i=1,\ldots,n}.

Corollary 3.10
.

As a consequence, if
T
fixes
V
, the set-theoretic support of
\operatorname{Spec}(H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}))
is
\mathfrak{t}\times\{0\}\subset\mathfrak{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}
.

Next, we state similar results for partial flag varieties of classical types. Here, we consider
\mathfrak{g}(V)
of classical types where the dimension of
V
is
n
,
2n
, or
2n+1
, respectively. Consider a composition of
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{l})
of
n
(so
\mu_{i}\geqslant 1
and
\mu_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{l}=n
). Write
\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
for the partial (isotropic) flag variety of
V
associated with
\mu
. In other words,
\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
parametrizes the chain of subspaces
F^{\bullet}=F^{1}\subset...\subset F^{l-1}\subset V
where
d_{i}:=\dim F^{i}=\mu_{1}+...+\mu_{i}
. The variety
\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
is isomorphic to
G(V)/P_{\mu}
where
P_{\mu}=GL({\mu_{1}})\times\ldots\times GL({\mu_{l-1}})\times G^{\prime}
for
G^{\prime}=GL(\mu_{l})
or
G^{\prime}
is of type
B_{\mu_{l}}
,
C_{\mu_{l}}
, or
D_{\mu_{l}}
. Recall that we write
\mathcal{F}
for the full flag variety, then we have a natural map
\pi_{\mu}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
. Write
M
and
W_{M}
for the Levi and the Weyl group of
P_{\mu}
, respectively.

Proposition 3.11
.

[
AF23
, Corollary 4.5.4]

The natural pullback
\pi_{\mu}^{*}\colon H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F}_{\mu})\rightarrow H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F})
is an injection of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]
-modules. The image of
\pi_{\mu}^{*}
is the
W_{M}
-invariant part of
H^{*}_{T}(\mathcal{F})
. In other words, we have the following explicit description.

(1)

Assume
\mathfrak{g}
is of type A. Over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]
, the image of this map is generated by
e_{i}(x_{{d_{j}}+1},\ldots,x_{d_{j+1}})
for
0\leqslant j\leqslant l-1
.

(2)

Assume
\mathfrak{g}
is of type B, C. Over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}]
, the image of this map is generated by
e_{i}(x_{{d_{j}}+1},\ldots,x_{d_{j+1}})
for
0\leqslant j\leqslant l-1
and
e_{i}(x^{2}_{{d_{l-1}}+1},\ldots,x^{2}_{d_{l}})
.

Similar to the case of full flag variety, the elements
e_{i}(x_{{d_{j}}+1},...,x_{d_{j+1}})
come from the corresponding tautological vector bundles
(F^{\bullet},F^{j+1}/F^{j})
.

3.5.
Fixed points of Slodowy varieties

For a nilpotent element
\chi\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, we have the corresponding Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}
and the Slodowy slice
S(\chi)
. Consider a torus
T\subset G
so that
T
centralizes the
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{2}
triple that we use to construct
S(\chi)
. Then
T
naturally acts on
S(\chi)
and
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}
. Write
\mathfrak{l}
for the centralizer of
T
in
\mathfrak{g}
. We have
\chi\in\mathfrak{l}
, and it makes sense to consider the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}(L)
. Recall that we write
\mathcal{B}_{L}
for the flag variety of
L
.

Proposition 3.12
.

The connected components of
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
are isomorphic to
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}(L)
. Each connected component of
\mathcal{B}^{T}
contains precisely one connected component of
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
. The inclusion
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{T}
can be viewed as
|W_{G}/W_{L}|
copies of the natural inclusion
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}(L)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}_{L}
.

Proof.

The variety
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
parametrizes the Borel subalgebras
\mathfrak{b}\subset\mathfrak{g}
that contain
\chi
. Because
\chi\in\mathfrak{l}
, we have
\chi\in\mathfrak{b}
if and only if
\chi\in\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{l}
. Therefore, the intersection of
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
and each connected component of
\mathcal{B}^{T}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}(L)
via the map
\mathfrak{b}\mapsto\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathfrak{l}
.

The Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{\chi}(L)
is connected (see
[
Spa82
]
). Therefore, we have a bijection between the connected components of
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
and the connected
\mathcal{B}^{T}
. The latter is parameterized by
W_{G}/W_{L}
by
Corollary

3.5
. ∎

Next, we further assume that
\chi
is regular in
\mathfrak{l}
. In this case, we have
S(\chi)^{T}=(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
.

Corollary 3.13
.

Each connected component of
(\mathcal{B}_{\chi})^{T}
(or
S(\chi)^{T}
) is a single point, and they are naturally labeled by
[w]\in W_{G}/W_{L}
.

A more general result for parabolic Slodowy variety
S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})
is as follows.

Proposition 3.14
.

S(\chi,\mathfrak{p})^{T}
is a discrete set of points, these points are labeled by the free
W_{L}
-
W_{M}
double cosets of
W_{G}
.

Proof.

See
Appendix

E
.
∎

4.
Conical symplectic singularities and symplectic duality

4.1.
Symplectic duality: basic properties

We mostly follow
[
KP21
]
in this section.
Let
X
be a conical symplectic singularity, and assume that the Poisson bracket on
X
has degree two. Let
\mathbb{C}[X]^{2}\subset\mathbb{C}[X]
be the degree two component, this is a Lie algebra w.r.t.
\{\,,\,\}
. Let
Z_{X}
and
T_{X}
be as in
Section

2.1
.

We assume the natural identification of Lie algebras
\operatorname{Lie}Z_{X}\simeq(\mathbb{C}[X]^{2},\{\,,\,\})
. Note that
[
Los22
, Proposition 2.14]
implies the identification if
\mathbb{C}[X]^{2}
is a reductive Lie algebra. Let
\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}=\operatorname{Hom}(T_{X},\mathbb{C}^{\times})
. The action of
T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
on
X
induces the bigrading
\mathbb{C}[X]=\bigoplus_{\mu\in\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{X,\mathbb{Z}},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathbb{C}[X]^{k}_{\mu}
. Note that
\mathbb{C}[X]^{2}_{0}
identifies with
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
.

Let
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
be the deformation of
\widetilde{X}
over
\mathfrak{h}_{X}=H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})
defined in
Section

2.1
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
be the canonical graded formal quantization of
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
,
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}
be the algebra of global sections, and let
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{2}
be the degree two component. There is an exact sequence of Lie algebras:

0\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[X]^{2}\rightarrow 0.

Passing to the zero weight space w.r.t.
T_{X}
we obtain the exact sequence:

(4.1.1)

0\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X},0}^{2}\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}_{X}\rightarrow 0.

Let us describe another exact sequence that one can associate to a symplectic singularity
X
. Let us assume that the
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
\widetilde{X}
is smooth (so we will denote it by
Y
). The odd cohomology of
Y
vanishes (
[
BPW16
, Proposition 2.5]
) so we have an exact sequence:

(4.1.2)

0\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\rightarrow H^{2}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}\rightarrow 0.

The first map is a composition of the pull back homomorphism
H^{2}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt},\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y,\mathbb{C})
and the identification
\mathfrak{t}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\simeq H^{2}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt},\mathbb{C})
. The second map is the restriction homomorphism
H^{2}_{T_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y,\mathbb{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{2}(Y,\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{h}_{X}
.

The basic idea of symplectic duality is that often conical symplectic singularities come in dual pairs
(X,X^{\vee})
. We refer the reader to
[
BLPW16
]
for details on symplectic duality. Let us recall some basic properties of it.
One prediction of symplectic duality (formulated in
[
KP21
, Section 5.1]
) is that for the dual pairs, exact sequences (
4.1.1
), (
4.1.2
) identify with each other: there exists a natural identification
\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X},0}\simeq H^{2}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee},\mathbb{C})
that induces the identifications:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X},0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{t}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{t}_{X^{\vee}}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee},\mathbb{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{X^{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

In particular, we should have canonical identifications

(4.1.3)

\mathfrak{h}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{t}_{X^{\vee}},~\mathfrak{t}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{X^{\vee}},

as was conjectured in
[
BLPW16
]
.

Recall now that in Section
2.1.4
we described stratifications of
\mathfrak{h}_{X}
,
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
into chambers separated by walls.
Another expectation of the symplectic duality is that the identifications (
4.1.3
) are compatible with the stratifications described in Section
2.1.4
. Namely, they induce the isomorphisms

(4.1.4)

\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{sing}}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{sing}}_{X^{\vee}},~\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{sing}}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{sing}}_{X^{\vee}}.

In particular, a choice of a generic cocharacter
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
determines a chamber in
\mathfrak{h}_{X,\mathbb{R}}\setminus\mathfrak{t}_{X,\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{sing}}
that by Proposition
2.4
has the form
w(\operatorname{Amp}(\widetilde{X}^{\vee}))
for some
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
\widetilde{X}^{\vee}\rightarrow X^{\vee}
and
w\in W_{X^{\vee}}
.

In this way, the choice of
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
determines a choice of a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
\widetilde{X}^{\vee}
on the dual side as well as the element
w\in W_{X^{\vee}}
. We will say that
\nu

corresponds
to
\widetilde{X}^{\vee}
and will use the identification
H^{2}(\widetilde{X}^{\vee,\mathrm{reg}},\mathbb{C})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathfrak{h}_{Y^{\vee}}
given by
\lambda\mapsto w(\lambda)
(i.e.,
\nu\in\operatorname{Amp}(\widetilde{X}^{\vee})
w.r.t. the identification above).

Assume now that both
X
and
X^{\vee}
admit symplectic resolutions.

Remark 4.1
.

It is expected that
X
has a symplectic resolution if and only if
(X^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}}
consists of
one
point.

The symplectic duality predicts the existence of a canonical identification of finite sets:

(4.1.5)

Y^{T_{X}}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}},~p\mapsto p^{\vee}.

4.2.
Hikita-Nakajima conjectures

Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X}
be a generic cocharacter meaning that the corresponding element of
\mathfrak{t}_{X}
is
not
contained in
\mathfrak{t}_{X}^{\mathrm{sing}}
. Let
Y^{\vee}\rightarrow X^{\vee}
be the symplectic resolution that corresponds to
\nu
in the sense of Section
4.1
.
We will always assume that
\nu
is “deep enough” in the ample chamber
\operatorname{Amp}(Y^{\vee})
. Note that by
[
BPW16
, Corollary B.1]
, this, in particular, implies that the abelian localization holds for
(\nu,Y^{\vee})
.

The following conjectures are natural to make (part (i) goes back to Hikita
[
Hik17
]
, and part (ii) goes back to Nakajima
[
Kam22
, Section 5.6]
). We will call (i)
Hikita conjecture
and (ii) will be called
equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture
. Recall that
X^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}
is the
schematic
fixed points of
X
(see Section
2.7.1
).

Conjecture 4.2
.

(i)

There is a graded algebra isomorphism
\mathbb{C}[X^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\simeq H^{*}(Y^{\vee})
;

(ii)

There is a graded isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
-algebras
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}})\simeq H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})
, where
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}})
is the Cartan subquotient with respect to a generic one-parameter subgroup
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}T_{X}
(corresponding to the resolution
Y^{\vee}
).

We remark that (ii) implies (i) by specifying to the point
0\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}
. Moreover, setting
\hbar=0
in (i), we obtain the identification

(4.2.1)

\mathbb{C}[X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{\nu(\mathbb{C}^{\times})}]\simeq H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}}(Y^{\vee})

that we will call
classical equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture
.
In what follows, we show that the conjecture as stated is not true and propose a refined version that we can prove in some cases and which implies Conjecture
4.2
in some cases.

Remark 4.3
.

Note that
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{X}^{\vee})
is a free
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
-module. In particular,
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{X}^{\vee})
is flat over
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
. However, that is not true for the left-hand side of (ii), a counterexample is described in
Example

6.7
. This is the first indication that the Hikita conjecture might be false.

Remark 4.4
.

It is still expected that Conjecture
4.2
holds as stated in the following case (this is the setting in which Nakajima formulated it in
[
Nak16
, Section 1(viii)]
). Let
(G_{\mathrm{gauge}},N)
be a pair of a reductive (gauge) group
G_{\mathrm{gauge}}
and a finite-dimensional
G_{\mathrm{gauge}}
-module
{\bf{N}}
. We can associate to
(G_{\mathrm{gauge}},N)
a pair of varieties

\mathcal{M}_{C}=\mathcal{M}_{C}(G_{\mathrm{gauge}},{\bf{N}}),~\mathcal{M}_{H}=\mathcal{M}_{H}(G_{\mathrm{gauge}},{\bf{N}})

called Coulomb and Higgs branches of the theory defined by
(G_{\mathrm{gauge}},{\bf{N}})
(for the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{C}
see
[
BFN18
, Section 3]
,
\mathcal{M}_{H}
is the Hamiltonian reduction of
T^{*}{\bf{N}}
by the action of
G_{\mathrm{gauge}}
). They are expected to be symplectic dual. We note that
\mathcal{M}_{C}
is always a symplectic singularity (see
[
Bel23
]
and
[
Wee22
]
). Assume that
\mathcal{M}_{H}
is a symplectic singularity.
Let
\mu\colon T^{*}{\bf{N}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{gauge}}^{*}
be a moment map for
G_{\mathrm{gauge}}\curvearrowright T^{*}{\bf{N}}
and assume that for a generic character
\xi\in(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{gauge}}/[\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{gauge}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{gauge}}])^{*}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathrm{gauge}}
, the action of
G_{\mathrm{gauge}}
on
\mu^{-1}(\xi)
is free, and that
\mu^{-1}(\xi)
is smooth. Then Conjecture
4.2
should hold for
X=\mathcal{M}_{C}
,
X^{\vee}=\mathcal{M}_{H}
.

5.
Refined BVLS duality and symplectic duality for parabolic Slodowy varieties

Recall that
\mathfrak{g}
is a simple Lie algebra, and
G
is the corresponding adjoint group. Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
,
G^{\vee}
be the Langlands dual Lie algebra and group, respectively. Pick the Cartan subalgebras
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. We start with recalling a standard Barbasch-Vogan-Lusztig-Spaltenstein (BVLS) duality.

Let
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}\subset(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{*}
be a nilpotent
G^{\vee}
-orbit, let
\chi^{\vee}\in\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
, and
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
be such that
\chi^{\vee}=(e^{\vee},\bullet)
. Pick an
\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{l}_{2}
-triple
(e^{\vee},f^{\vee},h^{\vee})
such that
h^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{*}
. Recall that the maximal ideals in
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
are in bijection with the central characters, and
Z(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}))\simeq S(\mathfrak{h})^{W}\simeq\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]^{W}
. Let
I(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})\subset\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
be the maximal ideal with the central character
\frac{1}{2}h^{\vee}
. The associated graded ideal
\gr I(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})\subset S(\mathfrak{g})
defines a closed subset
V\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, and there is a unique open
G
-orbit
\mathbb{O}\subset V
. Following
[
BV85
]
we define the BVLS duality map
D:\mathcal{N}^{\vee}/G^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{N}/G
by setting
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=\mathbb{O}
. The orbits in the image of the map
D
are called
special
. A key property of the map
D
is that it sends the Bala-Carter saturation to the Lusztig-Spaltenstein induction (see Sections
2.2
,
2.3
for the definitions). Namely, for a Levi
L^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}
and a nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L^{\vee}}^{\vee}\subset(\mathfrak{l}^{\vee})^{*}
we have:

D(\Sat_{L^{\vee}}^{G^{\vee}}\mathbb{O}_{L^{\vee}}^{\vee})=\Ind_{L}^{G}D(\mathbb{O}_{L^{\vee}}^{\vee}).

We proceed with the refined version of the map
D
, namely a map
\widetilde{D}
from the set of nilpotent orbits in
(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{*}
to the set of covers of nilpotent orbits in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Let
(L^{\vee},\mathbb{O}_{L^{\vee}}^{\vee})
be the Bala-Carter pair attached to the orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
. The orbit
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee}_{L^{\vee}})
is a special orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L}
in
\mathfrak{l}^{*}
, and let
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
be the universal
L
-equivariant cover of
\mathbb{O}_{L}
. We define
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}
. The following are the key properties of this map.

Proposition 5.1
.

[
LMM21
, Proposition 9.2.1]

(i)

\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is a
G
-equivariant cover of
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
.

(ii)

The map
\widetilde{D}
is injective.

(iii)

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the canonical filtered quantization of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]
. Note that it is endowed with a quantum comoment map
\Phi\colon\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}
. Then
\Ker(\Phi)=I(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
.

(iv)

For
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
distinguished, the cover
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is birationally rigid.

(v)

[
MMY
, Remark 1.3]
The Galois group of the cover
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is isomorphic to the Lusztig quotient
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
.

Recall that
S(\chi^{\vee})
is the Slodowy slice to
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
at the point
\chi^{\vee}
. Let

X^{\vee}=S(\chi^{\vee})\cap\mathcal{N}^{\vee},\,X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]).

For the orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
let
(L^{\vee},\mathbb{O}_{L^{\vee}}^{\vee})
be the Bala-Carter pair (see Proposition
2.8
).

Proposition 5.2
.

The following are true:

•

(i) For most orbits
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
we have
\mathfrak{h}_{X^{\vee}}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
;

•

(ii) For any orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
we have
\mathfrak{z}_{X^{\vee}}\simeq\mathfrak{z}_{G^{\vee}}(e^{\vee},f^{\vee},h^{\vee})
, and therefore
\mathfrak{z}_{X^{\vee}}\simeq\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{l}^{\vee})
,
T_{X^{\vee}}=Z({L^{\vee}})^{\circ}
(connected component of
1
in the center of
L^{\vee}
);

•

(iii) For most orbits
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
we have
\mathfrak{z}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{g}
, and therefore
\mathfrak{z}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}
;

•

(iv) For any orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
we have
\mathfrak{h}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})
.

Proof.

For (i), we refer to
Remark

2.15
. (ii) is well-known.
For (iii), we refer to
Proposition

2.13
. (iv) is shown in
[
LMM21
, Proposition 7.2.2(i)]
.
∎

A remark about “most” orbits is in order. For (i), we refer to
[
LNS11
, Theorem 1.3]
. The dual phenomena for (iii) is the one of “shared orbits” considered in
[
BK94
]
. The full list of shared orbits and shared covers is known, thanks to the results of
[
FJLS23
]
. In particular, one can check that
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
admits a shared cover for a group of a higher rank if and only if
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}
is one of the exceptions for (i).

We expect that
S(\chi^{\vee})\cap\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
and
\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
form a symplectic dual pair. In this paper we focus on a special case of the duality above. From now on we impose an additional assumption that the nilpotent
\chi^{\vee}\in\mathcal{N}^{\vee}
is regular for some Levi
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. Equivalently, this means that the corresponding orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}=G^{\vee}\chi^{\vee}
is the saturation of the regular nilpotent orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\vee}_{L^{\vee}}\subset(\mathfrak{l}^{\vee})^{*}
. Note that
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee}_{L^{\vee}})=\{0\}
is the zero orbit. We conclude that
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=\operatorname{Bind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}
can be described as follows. Recall that
P\subset G
is a parabolic with Levi
L
. Consider the natural morphism
\mu\colon T^{*}\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}
, the image of this morphism is the closure
\overline{\mathbb{O}}
of some orbit
\mathbb{O}
(recall that
\mathbb{O}=\operatorname{Ind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}
). Now,
\operatorname{Bind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}=\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}
is nothing else but
\mu^{-1}(\mathbb{O})
. We see that in this case
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
identifies with
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[T^{*}\mathcal{P}]
and so
Y=T^{*}\mathcal{P}
. We conclude that

Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee}),\quad Y=T^{*}\mathcal{P}

are symplectic dual. Note that both of them are particular cases of
parabolic
Slodowy varieties, namely we see that
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{b}^{\vee})=\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee})
is dual to
T^{*}\mathcal{P}=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{p})
.

This suggests a candidate for a symplectic dual to
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})
. It is natural to conjecture that

Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}),\,Y=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})

are symplectic dual.

Remark 5.3
.

Recall from
Remark

2.22
that the varieties
Y^{\vee}
,
Y
may be disconnected. In fact, the two examples presented are exactly of form
\widetilde{S}(p^{\vee},q^{\vee})
and
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
respectively for
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}
being parabolic subalgebras with Levi factors
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(1)^{2}\times\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(2)\subset\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(6)
and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}(3)\subset\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{p}(6)
respectively. We expect that
\widetilde{S}(p^{\vee},q^{\vee})
and
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})
always have the same number of connected components.

Warning 5.4
.

Strictly speaking, not
Y^{\vee}
and
Y
are dual but
X^{\vee}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})]
should be dual to
X=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})]
. There is a freedom in the choice of a resolution. We will later see that the natural choices are

Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}_{-},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}_{-}),~Y=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}).

Remark 5.5
.

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(n)
, then the symplectic duality between
