\textstyle{H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}((Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p\in Y^{T_{X}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{T_{X}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(Y))))}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

In particular, there exists the isomorphism of
B
-algebras:

(8.2.2)

\operatorname{Im}(B\rightarrow H^{*}_{T_{Y^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee}))\simeq\operatorname{Im}(B\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))\rightarrow\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{T_{X}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(Y)))).

Warning 8.5
.

Let us mention an important technical detail: for (
8.2.1
) to be commutative, one should
normalize
the map
a
by saying that
\hbar
goes to
\frac{1}{2}\hbar
(see, for example,
[
KTWWY19
, Conjecture 8.9]
or Theorem
9.5
below). This seemingly minor detail is crucial for applications to representation theory (see, for example, definition of
I({\mathbb{O}}^{\vee})
in Section
5
).

Remark 8.6
.

Let us mention one possible way to think about Conjecture
8.4
. We can consider
H^{*}_{T_{Y^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})
,
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X)
as
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
-modules of rank
|(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}}|=|Y^{T_{X}}|
.
The algebra
B
acts on both of these modules by
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
-linear endomorphisms. Let
\mathbb{K}
be the algebraic closure of the field of fractions
\operatorname{Frac}(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar])
. Base changing to
\mathbb{K}
, our modules become
|Y^{T_{X}}|
-dimensional vector spaces over
\mathbb{K}
, and they decompose into the direct sum of one-dimensional modules over
B_{\mathbb{K}}:=B\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]}\mathbb{K}
parametrized by certain characters of
B
(in other words, we diagonalize the action of
B_{\mathbb{K}}
on these modules). Conjecture
8.4
gives a concrete recipe to find these characters and claims that they should be the same.

8.2.2.
Main conjecture in the context of localization algebras

In
[
BLPPW11
, Definition 2.1]
, authors introduced a notion of a
localization algebra
, let us recall their definition.

Definition 8.7
.

A
localization algebra is a quadruple
\mathcal{Z}=(U,Z,\mathcal{I},h)
, where
U
is a finite-dimensional complex vector space,
Z
is a finitely generated graded
\operatorname{Sym}U
-algebra,
\mathcal{I}
is a finite set, and

h\colon Z\rightarrow\bigoplus_{p\in\mathcal{I}}\operatorname{Sym}U

is a homomorphism of
\operatorname{Sym}U
-algebras. If the kernel and cokernel are torsion
\operatorname{Sym}U
-modules, then we call
\mathcal{Z}
strong. If
Z
is free of rank
|\mathcal{I}|
as a
\operatorname{Sym}U
-module, we call
\mathcal{Z}
free.

We observe that algebras
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})
,
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
have
natural
structures of localization algebras. Indeed, for
Z=H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})
, as was already observed in
[
BLPPW11
, Example 2.2]
, we should take
U=\operatorname{Lie}(T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{*}
,
\mathcal{I}=(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}}
and
h=\iota^{*}
, where
\iota
is the embedding
(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\vee}
. This is an example of a
strong
and
free
localization algebra. Now, for
Z=\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
, we take
U=(\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C})^{*}
,
\mathcal{I}=Y^{T_{Y}}
, and
h
being the natural map
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))\rightarrow\Gamma(Y_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}^{T_{X}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(Y))))
. This is an example of a
strong
localization algebra.

Note now that our Conjecture
8.4
basically claims that the localization algebras structures above should be
compatible
.
In this sense, Conjecture
8.4
should be considered in the framework of the duality of “localization algebras”, see
[
BLPW16
, Section 10.6]
.

Let us mention that in
[
BLPW16
, Section 10.6]
,
[
BLPPW11
]
authors study another interesting example of the localization algebra, namely the (universal) deformation
Z(\widetilde{E})
of the center of the
geometric
category
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
for
Y^{\vee}
. In
[
BLPW16
, Conjecture 10.32]
authors conjecture that for
\lambda
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\lambda})
is
indecomposable
, localization algebras
Z(\widetilde{E})
,
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}}(Y^{\vee})|_{\lambda}
should be isomorphic. We
do not know
how to generalize this statement to arbitrary
\lambda
.

8.3.
Relation to
[
BDGHK18
]
and quantum Hikita-Nakajima conjecture

8.3.1.
Relation to physics

Let us now explain the motivation for Conjecture
8.4
coming from the paper
[
BDGHK18
]
by Bullimore, Dimofte, Gaiotto, Hilburn, and Kim. We restrict ourselves to the case
Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H}
,
X=\mathcal{M}_{C}
for a quiver gauge theory. Recall that
A
is a flavor torus.
Pick a
A
-fixed point
p\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H}^{A}
.
In
[
BDGHK18
]
, authors conjecture that (roughly speaking) the quantization
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}(\mathcal{M}_{C})
should act on the equivariant cohomology
H^{*}_{A\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{QMaps}_{p}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H}))
of the space of based quasi-maps sending
\infty
to
p
and the corresponding module should be the universal
point
module
\Theta_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}(p^{\vee})
over the quantized Coulomb branch. In general, it is not clear how to define the action of
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{a}}(\mathcal{M}_{C})
on the space above (see
[
Tam23
]
for the case of
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
). On the other hand, the action of
B=H^{*}_{G_{I}\times A\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
is easy to describe. Namely, consider the evaluation at
0
morphism
\operatorname{ev}_{0}\colon\operatorname{QMaps}_{p}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H})\rightarrow\mu^{-1}(0)/G_{I}
, then the element
\tau
should act via the multiplication by
\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\tau)
(compare with
[
Liu21
]
).

Now, the natural grading on the point module should correspond to the decomposition of the space
\operatorname{QMaps}_{p}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H})
via the degree of the quasi-map, let us denote the degree
d
component by
\operatorname{QMaps}_{p}^{d}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H})
. So, the highest weight component is nothing else but the cohomology of
\operatorname{QMaps}_{p}^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{H}):=\{p\}
. We conclude that the action of
\tau\in B
on the highest weight component
H^{*}_{A\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(p)=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{a},\hbar]
is given by the multiplication by
\iota_{p}^{*}\tau
. This is precisely what Conjecture
8.4
claims.

8.3.2.
Relation to the quantum Hikita-Nakajima conjecture

As we see from the discussion above, Conjecture
8.4
is a shadow of the (conjectural) realization of the universal point module over the Coulomb branch via the space of based quasi-maps to the Higgs branch. This statement should, in particular, imply that characters of (universal) point modules (considered as functions on
B
) coincide with the
\hbar=q
specializations of (normalized) vertex functions
with descendants
(introduced by Okounkov in
[
Oko17
, Section 7.2]
) restricted to the corresponding fixed points.
It turns out that this statement is precisely the quantum analog of Conjecture
8.4
and can be used to prove the quantum Hikita conjecture in some cases (the quantum Hikita conjecture was formulated in
[
KP21
]
). Namely, normalized vertex functions
with descendants are
solutions
of the “PSZ” quantum
D
-module while characters of point modules are solutions of the
D
-module of graded traces (compare with Remark
8.6
). By identifying solutions, we obtain identifications of the corresponding
D
-modules.
This is the joint work in progress of Hunter Dinkins, Ivan Karpov, and the second author. The relation of the quantum Hikita conjecture with the action of quantized Coulomb branches on the cohomology of the quasi-maps spaces was already observed in
[
HKW23
, Remark 1.10]
. We are grateful to Joel Kamnitzer for pointing this out.

8.4.
Refined Hikita-Nakjima conjecture vs Hikita-Nakajima conjecture

The natural question is: when Conjecture
8.4
implies the actual Hikita-Nakajima conjecture? Clearly, this is the case when morphisms
a,b
are surjective and
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
(note that
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee})
is always free over
H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\operatorname{pt})
).
The following claims hold.

(i)

Morphisms
a,b
are surjective if
Y^{\vee}
is a Nakajima quiver variety and
X
is the corresponding Coulomb branch (surjectivity of
a
follows from
[
MN18
, Corollary 1.5]
, for the surjectivity of
b
see
[
KS
, Proposition 8.7]
and references therein).

(ii)

Module
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
when
X
is a Coulomb branch of type
ADE
quiver corresponding to a framing vector
(w_{i})
and a dimension vector
(v_{i})
such that
w_{i}\neq 0
implies that
\omega_{i}
is minuscule and
\mu=\sum_{i}w_{i}\omega_{i}-\sum_{i}v_{i}\alpha_{i}
is dominant (follows from
[
KTWWY19
, Section 8.2]
). This also holds when
X
is a Coulomb branch of the Jordan quiver (see
[
KS
, Appendix A]
). Finally, we have flatness for
X=S(e^{\vee})
(see Proposition
7.11
).

(iii)

Morphism
a
is in general
not
surjective for
Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(e^{\vee})
, see Example
6.6

(iv)

Morphism
b
is in general
not
surjective. For example, consider
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{sp}({2n})
that corresponds to a
2
-row partition. Then
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is a double cover of an orbit
\mathbb{O}\subset\mathfrak{sp}_{2n+1}
that has the form
(3,2,...,2,1,...,1)
. This double cover can be realized as another nilpotent orbit in
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+2}
(
[
FJLS23
, Proposition 2.12]
). One can check that the map
b
is not surjective. In this case, it makes more sense to replace the polynomial algebra
B
that comes from
\mathfrak{so}({2n+1})
with the one that comes from
\mathfrak{so}_{2n+2}
.

(v)

Module
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
is in general
not
flat over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
for
X=\overline{\mathbb{O}}
, see Example
6.7
.

In general, we expect that
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
for any
quiver gauge theory
(see
[
KS
, Conjecture 8.3]
).
From the above, we already know that Conjecture
8.4
implies the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture when
X
is a Coulomb branch as in (ii). Proof of Conjecture
8.4
in this particular case can be deduced from the results of
[
KTWWY19
]
,
[
KTWWY19a
]
.

Overall, Conjecture
8.4
is a replacement of the
Hikita-Nakajima conjecture.

Remark 8.8
.

Let us now drop the assumption that
X
has a resolution of singularities. Recall that
\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
is the
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization corresponding to the choice of
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T_{X^{\vee}}
on the dual side. We still conjecture that there exists an isomorphism of algebras

\operatorname{Im}(S^{\bullet}(H^{2}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee}))\rightarrow H^{*}_{T_{X^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(Y^{\vee}))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Im}(S^{\bullet}(\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X},0}(X))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))\rightarrow\Gamma(\widetilde{X}^{T_{X}},\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(\widetilde{X}))).

A more refined version of this conjecture for
X^{\vee}=S(e^{\vee})
,
X=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]
is given in Section
10.4
.

9.
Refined equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})

The goal of this section is to prove Conjecture
8.4
for the pair

Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}),~Y=\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}),

where
\mathfrak{q}^{\vee},\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
are determined by
\mathfrak{p}
,
\mathfrak{q}
respectively using the natural bijection between roots of
\mathfrak{g}
and roots of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
.

9.1.
Restriction to fixed points for
T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
and
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})

Recall the restriction map
p\colon\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})=H^{2}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}_{-}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{2}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}_{-}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}_{-}^{\vee}))
.
By results of Section
7.1
, abelian localization theorem holds for
(p(\lambda),\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
if
\lambda\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{m})
is
\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}
-antidominant.
So, on the dual side, we should pick generic
\nu\colon\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow Z_{M}
that is
Q
-antidominant. By Proposition
E.7
, torus fixed points of
Y
are in the natural bijection with
(W_{M}\backslash W/W_{L})^{\mathrm{free}}
, and the fixed points of
Y^{\vee}
are in bijection with
(W_{L}\backslash W/W_{M})^{\mathrm{free}}
. The bijection of (
4.1.5
) is given by:

{\bf{i}}\colon Y^{T_{X}}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,(Y^{\vee})^{T_{X^{\vee}}},\,[w]\mapsto[w^{-1}].

Our goal is to compare the algebras
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))
,
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))
.

Without losing the generality, we can assume that
G^{\vee}
is simply connected. It follows that the derived subgroup of
M^{\vee}
is simply connected. Recall that the action of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
on
T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
given by
t\cdot(\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},x)=(\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},t^{-2}x)
.

As in Section
3.2
for a finite dimensional
M^{\vee}
-module
V
, let
\mathcal{V}
be the induced vector bundle
G^{\vee}\times^{Q^{\vee}}V\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
. Slightly abusing notations, we will denote by the same symbol the pullback of
\mathcal{V}
to
T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
.
Note that
\mathcal{V}
has a natural
T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant structure (actually, even
G^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant structure).
Let us describe the algebra
H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})
. Recall that by the Thom isomorphism, the pullback induces the isomorphism
H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})
.

It then follows from
Proposition

3.11
that

(9.1.1)

H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\longrightarrow}}}\,\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar],

sending
c_{i}(\mathcal{V})
to
e_{i}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
, where
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\in\mathfrak{h}=(\mathfrak{h}^{\vee})^{*}
are
\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
-weights (with multiplicities) that appear in
V^{*}
. Let

a\colon\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]

be given by
s(\lambda,\hbar)\otimes g(\lambda,\hbar)\mapsto[s(\lambda,\frac{1}{2}\hbar)\otimes g(\lambda,\frac{1}{2}\hbar)]
.

Lemma 9.1
.

The restriction homomorphism

(9.1.2)

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]=H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}((\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{T^{\vee}})=\bigoplus_{[w]\in W/W_{M}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]

is given by
s\otimes g\mapsto((w\cdot s)g)_{[w]\in W/W_{M}}
.

Proof.

The restriction homomorphism is clearly
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-linear, so we only need to compute the image of
c_{i}(\mathcal{V})\otimes 1
. Consider the natural morphism
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
,
the pull back of
\mathcal{V}
to
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
decomposes into the direct sum of line bundles, so it is enough to describe the character by which
T^{\vee}
acts on the fiber of a line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(-\lambda)=G^{\vee}\times_{B^{\vee}}\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}
(
\lambda\colon T^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
) restricted to a
T^{\vee}
-fixed point
wB^{\vee}/B^{\vee}
.

Fiber of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(-\lambda)
at
wB^{\vee}/B^{\vee}
identifies with
wB^{\vee}\times_{B^{\vee}}\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}
so for
t\in T^{\vee}
and
c\in\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}
we have:

t\cdot[w,c]=[tw,c]=[w(w^{-1}(t)),c]=[w,w^{-1}(t^{-1})\cdot c]=\lambda(w^{-1}(t))[w,c].

So the
T^{\vee}
-character of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\vee}}(-\lambda)|_{wB^{\vee}/B^{\vee}}
, considered as an element of
(\mathfrak{h}^{\vee})^{*}
is given by
\xi\mapsto\langle w^{-1}(\xi),\lambda\rangle=\langle\xi,w(\lambda)\rangle
so is equal to
w(\lambda)
.

It follows that
c_{i}(\mathcal{V})
maps to
e_{i}(w\lambda_{1},\ldots,w\lambda_{n})
, where
\lambda_{i}
are weights of
V^{*}
. Recall also that the identification (
9.1.1
) is given by
c_{i}(\mathcal{V})\mapsto e_{i}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
. So, we finally conclude that the map (
9.1.2
) sends
e_{i}(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\otimes 1
to the collection of functions
(e_{i}(w\lambda_{1},\ldots,w\lambda_{n}))_{[w]\in W/W_{M}}
. The claim follows.
∎

We have the embedding
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})\subset T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
.
Recall that the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})
is induced by the cocharacter
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,
t\mapsto(2\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}(t),t)
.

It still makes sense to consider a restriction (and forgetting the equivariance) homomorphism

\operatorname{res}\colon H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))

but it will
not
be
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-linear, the twist by
2\hbar\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}
appears (compare with Warning
2.31
).
Namely, consider the homomorphism
u\colon Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
given by
(g,t)\mapsto(2\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}(t)g,t)
. Let
\iota\colon\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})\hookrightarrow T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
be the embedding.
It follows from the definitions that for
p\in Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and
x\in\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})
we have
\iota(p\cdot x)=a(p)\cdot\iota(x)
. It follows that the homomorphism
\operatorname{res}
is indeed well-defined.
Let

j\colon\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]

be the map given by

f\mapsto((\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto f(\lambda+2\hbar_{0}\rho_{l},\hbar_{0})).

The following lemma holds by the definitons.

Lemma 9.2
.

For
x\in H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})
and
f\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]=H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})
, we have

\operatorname{res}(fx)=j(f)\operatorname{res}(x).

Proof.

Passing to the Lie algebras level, the homomorphism
u
induces the map

(9.1.3)

\mathfrak{z}(\mathfrak{l}^{\vee})\oplus\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}\oplus\mathbb{C},\,(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto(\lambda+2\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{l}},\hbar_{0}).

It remains to note that
j
is nothing else but the pullback homomorphism induced by the morphism (
9.1.3
).
∎

Corollary 9.3
.

The composition

(9.1.4)

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]=H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\rightarrow\\
\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}})=\bigoplus_{[w]\in(W_{L}\backslash W/W_{M})^{\mathrm{free}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]

is given by
[s\otimes g]\mapsto((\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto((w\cdot s)g)(\lambda+2\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{l}},\hbar_{0}))_{[w]\in(W_{L}\backslash W/W_{M})^{\mathrm{free}}}
.

Proof.

Let us, first of all, compute the image of
[s\otimes 1]
under (
9.1.4
). It follows from
Proposition

E.7
that we have the natural embedding
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}\subset(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{T^{\vee}}
.
Recall also that the embedding
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})\subset T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
is
Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant w.r.t. the homomorphism
u
.
Then it from Lemma
9.1
that the image of
[s\otimes 1]
is equal to

((\lambda,\hbar_{0})\mapsto(w\cdot s)(\lambda+2\hbar_{0}\rho_{\mathfrak{l}},\hbar_{0})_{[w]\in(W_{L}\backslash W/W_{M})^{\mathrm{free}}}.

Now, to determine the image of
[s\otimes g]
, it is enough to apply Lemma
9.2
.
∎

9.2.
Main result

Proposition 9.4
.

The following diagram is commutative (recall that the homomorphism
a
sends
\hbar
to
\frac{1}{2}\hbar
, and the identification of
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}
with
(W_{M}\backslash W/W_{L})^{\mathrm{free}}
is via
{\bf{i}}
):

\textstyle{H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}}
\textstyle{H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}}
\textstyle{H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{[w]\in(W_{M}\backslash W/W_{L})^{\mathrm{free}}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{b})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Gamma(\widetilde{S}_{\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})^{T_{e}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))))}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Proof.

Directly follows from Lemma
7.13
, and Corollary
9.3
.
∎

As an immediate corollary of Proposition
9.4
, we finally conclude.

Theorem 9.5
.

We have an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]
-algebras:

\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{T^{\vee}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}T^{*}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Im}(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p})))\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}))))

Proof.

The claim follows from Proposition
9.4
and the fact that the restriction homomorphism
H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}))\rightarrow H^{*}_{Z_{L^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p}^{\vee},\mathfrak{q}^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}})
is injective (use Lemma
2.6
).
∎

10.
Corollaries of refined Hikita, conjectures, and further directions

One of the goals of this section is to discuss the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for nilpotent orbits and Slodowy slices at various equivariant parameters. In other words, we consider
\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}=\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}
in Section 8. Recall that we discussed when the actual Hikita-Nakajima conjecture follows from our result after
Section

8.3.2
. A set of sufficient conditions includes that two morphisms
a,b
in
Conjecture

8.4
are surjective and
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
is flat over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
. Moreover, they are also necessary conditions for
Conjecture

1.1
in the case
\mathfrak{q}^{\vee}=\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}
(
[
H24a
, Proposition 1.5]
). Hence, in this section, we focus on these conditions.

It is noted that
b
is always surjective if we assume that the dual variety is a nilpotent orbit (instead of a nontrivial cover) with normal closure. All the algebras involved in the diagram in
Theorem

9.5
are algebras over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{X},\hbar]
.
We say that an equivariant parameter
(\lambda,t)\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
is
good
if
a|_{\lambda,t}
,
b|_{\lambda,t}
are surjective
and a condition (

♠

‣
Weak flatness condition
) holds for
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,t}(X))
, i.e.
\dim\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda,t}(X))=\dim\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda_{0},t_{0}}(X))
for a generic
(\lambda_{0},t_{0})
. If
(\lambda,t)
is good, the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture holds at this parameter.

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
be a classical simple Lie algebra. When
\mathfrak{g}
is of type A, the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture holds for the pair
\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{p}),\widetilde{S}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
. Details are given in
Appendix

D
. In this section, we assume that
\mathfrak{g}
is of type B, C or D. We focus on the equivariant parameters
(\lambda,t)=(0,0)
and
(\lambda,t)=(0,1)
. If
(0,0)
is good for a pair
(X^{\vee},X)
, then the classical Hikita conjecture holds. Moreover, the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture will also hold for this pair thanks to the graded Nakayama lemma (
Section

3.1
). The parameter
(0,1)
is related to the canonical quantization (see
[
LMM21
, Section 5]
) and has representation theoretic meaning. We also discuss a combinatorial application of our refined Hikita at this parameter in
Section

10.3
. In
Section

10.4
we discuss more general settings and give a heuristic explanation why the formulation of the Hikita conjecture needs further modifications.

10.1.
Classical Hikita conjecture for Slodowy slices and nilpotent orbits

Recall that
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is a nilpotent element such that
e^{\vee}
is regular in some Levi
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. Then
X^{\vee}
is
S(\chi^{\vee})
and
X
is the induced variety
\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}
(see Section 2.2 for the definition). Then
T_{X^{\vee}}
is the torus
T_{e^{\vee}}
.

First, we focus on the case when
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is a nilpotent orbit (not a cover) with a normal closure. These conditions automatically imply that the map
b
in
Equation

8.2.2
is surjective. Now, we consider the two other sufficient conditions for the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture.

In this setting, the map
a
in
Theorem

9.5
factors through the pullback in equivariant cohomology

(*)

i^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\colon H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}).

Hence, the surjectivity of
a|_{(0,0)}
is equivalent to the surjectivity of the pullback
i^{*}\colon H^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
. This condition often appears in the literature; see, e.g.,
[
KP12
, Theorem 1.2]
,
[
CK17
]
. Unfortunately, it is rarely satisfied when
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is not of type A.

Let
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
be the partition of
e^{\vee}
. Let
C_{G^{\vee}}(e^{\vee})
be the centralizer of
e^{\vee}
in
G^{\vee}
. Let
A^{\vee}=C_{G^{\vee}}(e^{\vee})/C^{o}_{G^{\vee}}(e^{\vee})
be the corresponding component group. The image of
i^{*}
is in
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{A^{\vee}}
. Hence, if
A^{\vee}
acts on
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
nontrivially,
i^{*}
is not surjective.

We first explain when the action of
A^{\vee}
on
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
is trivial. More details on the surjectivity of
i^{*}
are discussed in
Appendix

B
. Next, we restrict ourselves to the cases where
A^{\vee}
acts trivially on
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
and discuss the flatness of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
. We conclude with
Conjecture

10.4
that list certain cases where the classical Hikita conjecture (hence, the Nakajima-Hikita conjecture) is true.

10.1.1.
Surjectivity of the map
a

Let
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
be the partition of
e^{\vee}
. The criteria for
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
in classical types are specified in
Proposition

2.7
. We have the following lemma.

Lemma 10.1
.

Consider
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
of types B,C, and D. Let
(G^{\vee})^{ad}
be the corresponding adjoint group. Then

A^{\vee}
acts trivially on
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
if only if
(A^{\vee})^{ad}
is trivial. In particular, the partitions
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
are as follows.

(1)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{C}(2n)
,
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
has at most one even member, and if it has one, the multiplicity of this member is odd.

(2)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{B}(2n+1)
,
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
has exactly one odd member.

(3)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{D}(2n)
,
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
has at most two distinct odd members, and if it has two distinct odd members, their multiplicities are odd.

Proof.

This lemma is a direct consequence of the explicit Springer correspondence (see, e.g.,
[
Car93
, Section 13.3]
) and a result by Shoji. From
[
Sho83
, Theorem 2.5]
, a character of
A^{\vee}
appears in
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
if and only if it appears in
H^{top}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
. We explain which characters of
A^{\vee}
appears in
H^{top}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
for
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
of type C, other types are similar.

The following exposition follows from
[
Car93
, Section 13.3]
. From
\mathbf{p}
, we obtain a certain symbol
S_{\mathbf{p}}
that has two rows, each consisting of non-negative integers. This symbol
S_{\mathbf{p}}
has the following property.

(*)

The difference between any two distinct entries in the same row is at least
2
.

The characters of
A^{\vee}
in
H^{top}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
correspond to the permutations of
S_{\mathbf{p}}
that satisfy (*). Hence, for
A^{\vee}
to act trivially on
H^{top}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
, we want
S_{\mathbf{p}}
to have no other such permutation. The nonzero entries that only appear once in
S_{\mathbf{p}}
form a set
S
. Write
S
as the disjoint union of maximal intervals of the form
i,i+1,...,j
for
i<j
. Because
S_{\mathbf{p}}
has property (*), the odd entries of such an interval belong to one row of
S_{\mathbf{p}}
, and the even entries belong to the other row. As a consequence, the condition that
S_{\mathbf{p}}
does not have nontrivial permutations that satisfy (*) is equivalent to the condition that
S
itself is an interval of odd lengths or
S
is empty. From
[
Car93
, Section 13.3]
, these conditions are equivalent to
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
having precisely one even member of odd multiplicity or
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
having no even member.

∎

Remark 10.2
.

Even when
A^{\vee}
acts trivially on
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
, one should not expect the pullback
i^{*}
to be surjective in general. Details are given in
Appendix

B
.

10.1.2.
Flatness of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))

In this subsection we restrict ourselves to the case where the partition
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
of
e^{\vee}
satisfies the conditions in
Lemma

10.1
. The next lemma describes partitions
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\widetilde{D}(G^{\vee}.e^{\vee})
is an orbit in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
with normal closure. Recall that we assume that
e^{\vee}
is regular in
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
, and therefore
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}(\{0\})
.

Lemma 10.3
.

For
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
in
Lemma

10.1
, the partitions of the following forms satisfy that
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is a nilpotent orbit with normal closure.

(1)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{C}(2n)
,
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
is of the form
((2a)^{d_{0}},2b_{1}+1\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k}+1)
where
d_{0}
odd and
a\geqslant b_{1}
.

(2)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{B}(2n+1)
,
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
is of the form
((2a+1)^{d_{0}},2b_{1}\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k})
where
d_{0}
odd and
a\leqslant b_{k}
.

(3)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}\in\mathcal{P}_{D}(2n)
, we have two cases:

(a)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
is of the form
((2a+1)^{d_{0}},2b_{1}\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k},1^{d})
where
d_{0},d
odd and
a+1\geqslant b_{1}
.

(b)

\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
consists only of even members and has at most two distinct members.

The partitions above may not be written in the standard order.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for the case
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}=\mathfrak{sp}({2n})
, the other cases are similar. First, for the partition
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
in (1) of
Lemma

10.1
, the group
(A^{\vee})^{ad}
is trivial. It follows that the Lusztig quotient
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is trivial. Recall from
Proposition

5.1
(v) that
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is isomorphic to the Galois group of the cover
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})\rightarrow D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. Therefore,
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
.

Next, we show that the closure of
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is normal if and only if
a\geqslant b_{1}
. Let
\mathbf{p}
be the partition of
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. Write
e(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})
for
((2a)^{d_{0}},2b_{1}+1\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k}+1,1)
. Then
\mathbf{p}
is
B(e(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{t})
in which B denotes the B-collapse (for more details, see, e.g.,
[
LMM21
, Section 3.2]
).

If
a\geqslant b_{1}
, the above process gives
p=

(2a+1,(2a)^{d_{0}-1},2b_{1}+1\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k}+1)^{t}
. Then all members of
\mathbf{p}
are odd, so the closure of
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is normal (see, e.g.,
[
Won13
, Section 3]
,
[
KP82
]
).

Consider
a<b_{1}
. Let
2l_{1}
be the multiplicity of
b_{1}
in
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
. If
a=0
, then
e(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{t}
is already a partition of type B. If
a>0
, then
e(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{t}
is of the form
(y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{2b_{1}+1})
in which
y_{1}=y_{2}+1
is even,
y_{2j+1}=y_{2j+2}
odd for
j\leqslant a-1
,
y_{2a+1}=y_{2a+2}+1
is even. Hence, the B-collapse of
e(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{t}
becomes
(y_{1}-1,y_{2},y_{3},y_{4},
...,y_{2a+1},y_{2a+2}+1,y_{2a+3},
...,y_{2b_{1}+1})
. In either case, the first
2l_{1}
columns of the Young diagram of
\mathbf{p}
has length
2b_{1}+1
, and the
2l+1
-th column has length
max(2b_{l+1}+1,2a+1)<2b_{1}+1
. Consequently, the closure of
D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is not normal (see, e.g.
[
Won13
, Theorem 3.3]
,
[
KP82
]
). ∎

10.1.3.
Summary

We have seen in
Proposition

6.9
that the flatness condition of
Section

6.2
fails in the case
\mathfrak{l}
is a Levi subalgebra of
\mathfrak{sp}({2n})
that contains a nontrivial
\mathfrak{sp}
factor. Therefore, it fails for
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
in (2) of
Lemma

10.3
when
a>0
. When
a=0
, a necessary condition for
i^{*}
to be surjective is that
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
takes the form
(2^{2l},1^{2n+1-4l})
(
Theorem

B.2
). The situation is slightly less restrictive for the other two cases. In the following, we list the cases where we expect the parameter
(0,0)
to be good.

Conjecture 10.4
.

Let
e^{\vee}
be a regular nilpotent element in a Levi
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. The Hikita-Nakajima conjecture holds for the pair
(X^{\vee}=S(\chi^{\vee})
,
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}\{0\}]))
in the following cases.

(1)

G=Sp(2n)
,
L
takes the form
\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL({2})\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l}GL({1})
.

(2)

G=SO(2n+1)
,
L
takes the form
SO(3)\times\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL({2})\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l-1}GL({1})
,
SO(5)\times\prod GL(3)
, or
SO(2l+1)\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l}GL({1})
.

(3)

G=SO(2n)
,
L
takes the form
SO(4)\times\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL({2})\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l-2}GL({1})
,
SO(2)\times\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL({2})\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l-1}GL({1})
,
SO(2l)\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l}GL({1})
, of
L=GL(n)
if
n
is even.

We do not claim that these are all the cases where the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture holds for this type of pairs. Let us briefly explain the situations for the cases in
Conjecture

10.4
. The surjectivity of the pullback map is proved for
L=SO(2l+1)\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l}GL(1)
in
Proposition

B.8
, other cases are predicted in
Conjecture

B.7
. The weak flatness condition (

♠

‣
Weak flatness condition
) holds for the case
G=Sp(2n)
, and
L=\prod_{i=1}^{l}GL(2)\times\prod_{j=1}^{n-l}GL(1)
(see
Remark

6.10
). The weak flatness condition (

♠

‣
Weak flatness condition
) for
G
is of type B, D and other statements related to this conjecture are studied in
[
H24a
]
.

10.2.
Other parameters

This section discusses Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for nilpotent orbits and Slodowy slices at
(\lambda,t)\neq(0,0)
. We recall the following basic result in equivariant cohomology.

Consider a torus
T
and a
T
-equivariant morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
. Let
\mathfrak{t}
be the Lie algebra of
T
, and
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}
,
\lambda\neq 0
. The map
f^{*}_{T}:H^{*}_{T}(Y)\rightarrow H^{*}_{T}(X)
is a morphism of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}]
-algebras. From the discussion in
Section

3.1.1
, the specialization of
f_{T}^{*}
at
\lambda
can be identified with
H^{*}(Y^{\lambda})\rightarrow H^{*}(X^{\lambda})
where
X^{\lambda}
and
Y^{\lambda}
are the fixed point subvarieties of the vector fields induced by
\lambda
.

Let
(\lambda,t)\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
be a general parameter. If
t\neq 0
, we can replace
(\lambda,t)
by
(\lambda/t,1)
, see the discussion before
Equation

2.7.3
. Therefore, we can assume that
t
is either
0
or
1
.

We first consider
(\lambda,0)
for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
,
\lambda\neq 0
. We explain how to reduce this case to the parameter
(0,0)
for a certain (smaller) pair
(X^{\prime\vee}\subset(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee})^{*},X^{\prime})
where
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
is of the same type as
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. Our reduction argument uses the result that the Nakajima-Hikita conjecture holds in type A (
Appendix

D
).

Write
(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\lambda}
for the fixed point varieties of
\mathcal{B}^{\vee}
and
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
under the action of the vector field induced by
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}=\mathfrak{t}_{e^{\vee}}
. At
(\lambda,0)
, the pullback map in (
*
‣
10.1
) becomes

i^{*}_{(\lambda,0)}:H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\lambda})\rightarrow H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\lambda}).

Let
L^{\vee}_{\lambda}\subset G^{\vee}
be the centralizer of
\lambda
and write
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}
for its Lie algebra. Since
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}_{e^{\vee}}
, we have
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}\supset\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
. Consider
e^{\vee}
as a nilpotent element of
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}
. By
Proposition

3.12
, the question about the surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(\lambda,0)}
for
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
reduces to the question about surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(0,0)}
for
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}
.

Next, the Levi subalgebra
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
has a decomposition of the form
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathfrak{gl}_{\lambda_{i}}
where
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
is of the same type as
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. Correspondingly, we can decompose
e^{\vee}
as
(e^{\prime\vee},e^{\vee}_{1},...,e^{\vee}_{m})
for
e^{\prime\vee}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
and
e^{\vee}_{i}\subset\mathfrak{gl}_{\lambda_{i}}
. Note that
e^{\prime\vee}
is regular in
\mathfrak{l}^{\prime\vee}=\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}\cap\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
. Let
\mathcal{B}_{i}
be the flag variety for
\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}_{i}
. Note that all the pullback maps
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{i})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}_{i}})
are surjective. Therefore, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 10.5
.

The specialized map
i^{*}_{\lambda,0}
is surjective if and only if
(i^{\prime})^{*}\colon H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\prime})^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{(e^{\vee})^{\prime}})
is surjective.

A similar phenomenon appears on the side of
X\subset\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Write
\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
for the Langlands dual of
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}_{\lambda}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
respectively. Let
L_{\lambda}
and
G^{\prime}\subset L_{\lambda}
be the corresponding subgroups. In the setting of Section 9.1,
X
is a nilpotent orbit with normal closure in
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Recall that we have the deformation
\pi:X_{\mathfrak{h}_{X}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}
. The algebra
\mathbb{C}[\pi^{-1}(\lambda)]
is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on a coadjoint orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
(see proof of
[
Los22
, Proposition 4.5]
). We describe the orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
. Under an identification
\mathfrak{t}_{e^{\vee}}=\mathfrak{h}_{X}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, we obtain an element
c_{\lambda}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
that corresponds to
\lambda\in\mathfrak{t}_{e^{\vee}}
. Then the orbit
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
can be realized as
G\times^{L_{\lambda}}(c_{\lambda}+\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{L_{\lambda}}\{0\})
.

View the orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L_{\lambda}}:=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{L_{\lambda}}\{0\}
as
\tilde{D}(L^{\vee}_{\lambda}.e^{\vee})
. Write
\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}
as
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathfrak{gl}_{\lambda_{i}}
. This expression is compatible with the refined BVLS duality, so we can decompose the orbit
\mathbb{O}_{L_{\lambda}}
as
\tilde{D}(e^{\prime\vee})\times\tilde{D}(e^{\vee}_{1})\times\ldots\times\tilde{D}(e^{\vee}_{m})
. Because orbit closures are normal in
\mathfrak{gl}_{\lambda_{i}}
, the normality of
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L_{\lambda}}\subset\mathfrak{l}_{\lambda}^{*}
depends only on the normality of
\overline{\tilde{D}(e^{\prime\vee})}
in
(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})^{*}
.

Assume that
e^{\vee}
has partition
\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}
as in
Lemma

10.3
.
Then the partition
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime\vee}
of
e^{\prime\vee}
is a subpartition of
\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}
, hence satisfies the condition in
Lemma

10.3
.
Hence, the closure of the orbit
\widetilde{D}(e^{\prime\vee})
in
(\mathfrak{g}^{\prime})^{*}
is normal, and thus
\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{L_{\lambda}}
is normal.
This implies the normality of
\mathbb{O}^{\prime}_{\lambda}
(see
[
Hes79
, Section 1]
). Thus, the weak flatness condition (

♠

‣
Weak flatness condition
) at
(\lambda,0)
becomes the weak flatness condition at
(0,0)
for
e\in L_{\lambda}
. In conclusion, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 10.6
.

The Hikita-Nakajima conjecture is true for the pair
(X^{\vee}=S({\chi^{\vee}}),X=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}(\{0\}))
at a parameter
(\lambda,0)
if and only if it is true for the pair
(X^{\vee}=S{(\chi^{\prime\vee})},X=\mathrm{Bind}_{L^{\prime}}^{G^{\prime}}(\{0\}))
at
(0,0)
.

In particular, consider
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}
such that the projection of
\lambda
to each factor
\mathfrak{gl}
of
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
is nonzero. Then
e^{\prime\vee}
is regular in
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime\vee}
, and we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 10.7
.

For such
\lambda
, the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture for
(X^{\vee}=S_{\chi^{\vee}},X=\mathrm{Bind}_{L}^{G}(\{0\}))
holds at
(\lambda,0)
.

Remark 10.8
.

For
\lambda\neq 0
, the validity of Hikita-Nakajima conjecture at
(\lambda,0)
does not imply its validity at
(\lambda,t)
,
t\neq 0
.

Next, we consider the Hikita-Nakajima conjecture at
(\lambda,t)=(0,1)\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
. At this parameter, the pullback map in (
*
‣
10.1
) becomes

i^{*}_{(0,1)}:H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\rightarrow H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}).

Regarding this map, we have the following lemma and a more general conjecture.

Lemma 10.9
.

The triviality of the action of the component group is sufficient for the surjectivity of this pullback map. In other words,
i^{*}_{(0,1)}
is surjective for
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
described in
Lemma

10.1
.

Sketch of proof.

The proof is technical and we are not going to use the result later on, so we give a sketch. Assume
\mathfrak{g}
is of type C and
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
takes the form
((2a)^{d_{0}},2b_{1}+1\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k}+1)
where
d_{0}
odd if
a\neq 0
. Each connected component of
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is then isomorphic to the product
(\mathcal{B}_{e_{0}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\times(\mathcal{B}_{e_{1}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
where
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
have partitions
((2a)^{d_{0}})
and
(2b_{1}+1\geqslant...\geqslant 2b_{k}+1)
, respectively (see
Proposition

3.12
). Hence, it is enough to work with the case where the members of
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
have the same parity.

Step 1
. Consider the case where
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
has all parts equal. Let
k
be the number of parts in
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
. Each connected component of
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is a tower of projective bundles over an orthogonal (or symplectic) partial flag variety of
\mathbb{C}^{k}
(
[
H24
, Section 8.3]
). A symplectic partial flag variety is again a tower of projective bundles over a point. An orthogonal partial flag variety of
\mathbb{C}^{k}
can be viewed as a tower of odd quadric over a point. The result then follows from the fact that the cohomology rings (with complex coefficients) of odd quadrics and projective spaces are generated by their second degree components (
[
EG95
]
).

Step 2
. Consider the case where
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
consists of only odd members. Let
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
be
(2b_{1}+1,...,2b_{k}+1)
for
b_{1}\geqslant b_{2}\geqslant...\geqslant b_{k}\geqslant 0
. We prove the surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(0,1)}
by induction on the number of distinct parts of
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
. The base case that
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
has equal parts was proved in the previous step. Similarly to
Proposition

B.4
, the surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(0,1)}
for
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
would follow from the surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(0,1)}
for
(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{i}
where
(\mathbf{p}^{\vee})^{i}
is obtained from
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}
by replacing a pair
(2b_{i}+1=2b_{i+1}+1)
by
(2b_{i}+3=2b_{i+1}+3)
for some
1\leqslant i\leqslant k-1
. After a finite number of such steps, we will arrive at the case of a partition with fewer distinct parts, and the surjectivity follows from the induction hypothesis.

∎

Moreover, we conjecture the following.

Conjecture 10.10
.

The image of
i^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is the
A^{\vee}
-invariant part of
H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})
.

We make some comments about the parameters
(\lambda,1)
for general
\lambda
. First, consider the vector field induced by
(\lambda,1)
and the fixed point varieties
(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{(\lambda,1)}
,
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{(\lambda,1)}
. At
(\lambda,1)
, the pullback map in (
*
‣
10.1
) becomes

i^{*}_{(\lambda,1)}:H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{(\lambda,1)})\rightarrow H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{(\lambda,1)}).

Recall that we write
T_{\lambda}^{\vee}
for the torus with Lie algebra
\mathbb{C}(\lambda,0)
. Outside of a
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
\mathfrak{h}_{{e}^{\vee}}
, the variety
H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{(\lambda,1)})
is the simultaneous fixed point of
T_{\lambda}^{\vee}
and
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. In this case, we have a result similar to
Proposition

10.6
.

At the parameters
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{{e}^{\vee}}
where
(\lambda,1)
is not generic in
\mathbb{C}\lambda\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
, the behavior of the map
i^{*}_{(\lambda,1)}
may exhibit interesting features. For example,
i^{*}_{(\lambda,1)}
may not be surjective even when both
i^{*}_{(0,1)}
and
i^{*}_{(\lambda,0)}
are surjective. As an application, in
Appendix

B
, we use a certain parameter
(\lambda,1)
to prove the failure of the surjectivity of
i^{*}_{(0,0)}
(see
Lemma

B.5
).

10.3.
A combinatorial application: Springer fibers and a left cell

In this section, we explain a combinatorial application of our refined Hikita conjecture. Our aim is to produce a bijection between two sets of somewhat different natures. The first set consists of the element in a left cell in a (integral) Weyl group. This set appears as the parametrization of simple modules in the BGG category
\mathcal{O}
that are annihilated by a certain primitive ideal. The second set describes the
A^{\vee}
-orbits of irreducible components of certain fixed point loci of the Springer fibers.

We specialize all the algebras in the diagram of
Proposition

9.4
to
(\lambda,t)=(\lambda,1)\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hbar
and obtain two maps of algebras.

\psi_{\lambda}:\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]\rightarrow H_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})_{(\lambda,1)}\rightarrow H_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})_{(\lambda,1)}\rightarrow\oplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}},

\Psi_{\lambda}:\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}]\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{b_{(\lambda,1)}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))\rightarrow\oplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}.

From
Proposition

9.4
we can identify the sources, targets, and the images of these two maps. Therefore, we obtain an isomorphism between the two algebras
B_{1,\lambda}=\operatorname{Im}(\Psi_{\lambda})
and
B_{2,\lambda}=\operatorname{Im}(\psi_{\lambda})
.

The algebra
B_{1,\lambda}
is determined by the image of
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]
. For each
[w]\in W/W_{L}
, let
\mathbb{C}_{[w]}
denote the corresponding summand in
\oplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C})
. By the proof of
Lemma

7.13
, the map
\mathfrak{h}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{[w]}
records the highest weight of a simple
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))
-modules. View
\Spec(B_{1,\lambda})
as a subscheme of
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, then
\Spec(B_{1,\lambda})
is supported on a finite set
\Supp_{\lambda}
.

Next, we relate
\Supp_{0}
to a fixed point variety of the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
. Let
Y_{(0,1)}
be the set of
A^{\vee}
-orbits of the connected components of
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
.

Proposition 10.11
.

The isomorphism
B_{2,0}\simeq B_{1,0}
from
Proposition

9.4
induces a bijection between the two sets
\Supp_{0}
and
Y_{(0,1)}
.

Proof.

First, we view
\Supp_{0}
as the set-theoretic support of
\Spec(B_{2,0})
. Here,
\Spec(B_{2,0})
is naturally a subscheme of
\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}
. Similarly to Section
10.2
, we have

B_{2,0}=\operatorname{Im}\left(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}]\rightarrow H^{*}((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\xrightarrow{i^{*}_{0,1}}H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\xrightarrow{j^{*}_{0}}\oplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C}\right).

Let
X_{\alpha}
be a connected component of
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}
be the corresponding connected component of
(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
that contains
X_{\alpha}
. From
Lemma

3.6
, we know that
\Spec(H^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}))
is set-theoretically supported on a single point and for two components
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}\neq\mathcal{F}_{\beta}
of
(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
, the corresponding supports are different.

From
[
DLP88
, Section 3.7]
and
[
DLP88
, Section 2.1]
, we deduce that
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{F}_{\beta}
if and only if
X_{\alpha}
and
X_{\beta}
are in the same
A^{\vee}
-orbit. Write
\Con((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}))
for the set of connected components of
(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
. We have an identification

(**)

Y_{(0,1)}=\{\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}\in\Con((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}),i^{*}_{0,1}(H^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\alpha}))\neq 0\}.

For any nonzero quotient
B_{\alpha}
of
H^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\alpha})
, the scheme
\Spec(B_{\alpha})
is supported at a single point. Hence,
\Supp_{0}
, the support of
B_{2,0}
is identified with
\{\mathcal{F}\in\Con((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}),j_{0}^{*}\circ i^{*}_{0,1}(H^{*}(\mathcal{F}))\neq 0\}
. Now, recall that the map
H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\xrightarrow{j^{*}_{0}}\bigoplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C})
is the pullback
H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\rightarrow H^{*}((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{T_{e^{\vee}}})
. Each connected component
X_{\alpha}
of
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is smooth and projective. Hence,
X_{\alpha}
contains at least one point fixed by
T_{e}{{}^{\vee}}
. Therefore,
j_{0}^{*}\circ i^{*}_{0,1}(H^{*}(\mathcal{F}))\neq 0
is equivalent to
i^{*}_{0,1}(H^{*}(\mathcal{F}))\neq 0
. Combining with (
**
‣
10.3
), we obtain the desired bijection.
∎

Remark 10.12
.

For a general
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}_{X}
, let
T^{\vee}_{\lambda,1}\subset T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the torus with Lie algebra
\mathbb{C}(\lambda,1)
. The action of
A^{\vee}
and the action of
T^{\vee}_{\lambda,1}
do not commute. Hence, we need to define the set
Y_{(\lambda,1)}
in another way.

Consider an equivalence relation on the set
\Con((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{T^{\vee}_{\lambda,1}})
defined as follows. We say
X_{\alpha}
and
X_{\beta}
belong to the same class if
X_{\alpha}
and
X_{\beta}
both lie in a connected component
\mathcal{F}_{\gamma}
of
\Con((\mathcal{B}^{\vee})^{T^{\vee}_{\lambda,1}})
. Let
Y_{(\lambda,1)}
be the set of equivalence class in
\Con((\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{T^{\vee}_{\lambda,1}})
with respect to this relation. Then the proof
Proposition

10.11
works similarly, and we have a bijection between
Y_{(\lambda,1)}
and
\Supp_{\lambda}
.

Remark 10.13
.

Note that we have obtained the bijections between
\Supp_{\lambda}
and
Y_{(\lambda,1)}
without computing either set. This is a notable strength of our deformation approach since only explicit computation is needed at the generic
(\lambda,t)
for the proof of
Proposition

9.4
. On the other hand, these bijections at certain special parameters
(\lambda,1)
have interesting nontrivial combinatorial interpretations. In the rest of this section, we discuss a corollary of
Proposition

10.11
.

Assume
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is an orbit in
\mathfrak{g}
with normal closure. Then the map
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{b_{(\lambda,1)}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))
is surjective. Hence, the set
\Supp_{\lambda}
is the set of the highest weights of the simple modules of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))
in the corresponding category
\mathcal{O}_{\nu}
.

Write
I_{\mathfrak{p},\lambda}
for the kernel of the map
b_{(\lambda,1)}
above. From the proof of
Lemma

7.13
, the simples of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,\lambda}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))
are in bijection with the simples annihilated by
I_{\mathfrak{p},\lambda}
of the BGG category
\mathcal{O}
for
\mathcal{U}_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{g})
. These simple modules are parameterized by a certain subset
c_{P,\lambda}\subset W
. In particular, each element
w\in c_{P,\lambda}
corresponds to the simple module
L(w.\frac{1}{2}(\rho_{\mathfrak{l}}-\rho))
of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
. Here, the action of
w
is the
\rho
-shifted action. For
\lambda=0
, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 10.14
.

When
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is an orbit in
\mathfrak{g}
with normal closure, we have a bijection
Y_{(0,1)}\simeq c_{P,0}
.

Remark 10.15
.

Assume
e^{\vee}
is even. The bijection in
Corollary

10.14
can be made more precise as follows. The set
Y_{(0,1)}
of
A^{\vee}
-orbits of connected components of
(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
admits a parametrization by the cosets
W_{\mathfrak{p}^{\vee}}w
such that
P^{\vee}wB^{\vee}/B^{\vee}\cap\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
nonempty. If we pick out the longest elements in those cosets, they are precisely the elements that belong to
c_{P,0}
.

Recall that
Corollary

10.14
requires the hypothesis that
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is the orbit
\mathbb{O}=D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
of
\mathfrak{g}
. If we drop this assumption, the map
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,0}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{b_{(0,1)}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,0}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))
may not be surjective. Nevertheless, the map
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,0}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{b_{(0,1)}}\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{1,0}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))\rightarrow\oplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C}
still records the highest weights of certain simple
(\mathcal{A}_{1,0}(T^{*}\mathcal{P}))^{\Pi}
-modules where
\Pi=\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})
(see
Remark

2.29
). Therefore, we have an injection
Y_{(0,1)}=\Supp_{0}\hookrightarrow c_{P,0}
.

An explicit combinatorial construction of this injection (as well as the generalization of this story to the parabolic setting) will be presented in another work.

Let us finish this section by mentioning an interesting relation of the combinatorial bijection obtained in
10.11
and the results and predictions of a recent preprint
[
SXY23
]
(see also
[
PY
]
). In loc. cit, the authors propose a mirror symmetry between vertex operator algebras arising (via the
4d/2d
correspondence) from some
4d

\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal field theories and the Coulomb branches of
3d
theories obtained by compactifying the
4d
theories above on the circle.
One of the main conjectures of the paper (see point 1 of the introduction in loc. cit.) claims that there should be a bijection between irreducible objects in category
\mathcal{O}
for certain affine vertex algebras and
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-fixed points of certain affine Springer fibers. This looks similar to the bijection
10.11
. One might hope that
there is an analog of the equivariant Hikita-Nakajima conjecture in this setting that would imply and give a conceptual explanation of the above conjecture.

10.4.
More general settings

10.4.1.
Distinguished case

So far, we have been considering the Nakajima-Hikita conjecture for the pair
X^{\vee}=S_{e^{\vee}}
and
X=

\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}])
for
e^{\vee}
regular in some Levi and
\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}=\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. In this section, we propose a conjecture for the “opposite” setting. In particular, we assume that
e^{\vee}
is a distinguished nilpotent element of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. In this setting, the torus
T_{X^{\vee}}
is trivial. Recall that by
Proposition

5.1
(v)
\Aut_{\mathbb{O}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{O}})\simeq\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. Thus, the following two important conditions are relaxed.

(1)

X=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]
no longer admits a symplectic resolution.

(2)

The Lusztig quotient
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is rarely nontrivial. In other words, we no longer assume that
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
.

Recall that we write
A^{\vee}
for the component group of
C_{G^{\vee}}(e^{\vee})
. Let
A^{\prime}
be the kernel of the map
A^{\vee}\twoheadrightarrow\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. We expect the following:

Conjecture 10.16
.

Assume that
e^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is a distinguished nilpotent element, and let
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
. Then there is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]
-algebras

H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]

that is compatible with the action of
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
on both sides.

Warning 10.17
.

We only propose this statement for distinguished
e^{\vee}
. For example, the statement in
Conjecture

10.16
is wrong when
e^{\vee}
is regular in some Levi
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}
,
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
trivial, and
A^{\vee}
non-trivial. In this case, the right side of
Conjecture

10.16
is a module over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{\pm}]
of generic rank
|W/W_{L}|
while the rank of the left side is strictly smaller.

Note that Conjecture
10.16
is equivalent to the existence of
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-equivariant isomorphism of algebras:

(10.4.1)

H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})^{A^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X)).

Passing to
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-invariants, we then expect the identification:

(10.4.2)

H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})^{A^{\vee}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}.

Actually, we expect that the identification (
10.4.2
) holds for
arbitrary
nilpotent
e^{\vee}
, where on the RHS we consider the
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-invariants of the Cartan subquotient of the quantization of
X
with period
0
.
Moreover, we expect that both of the algebras should be quotients of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]
, so the identification (
10.4.2
) would reduce to the equality kernels.
It actually follows from
[
LMM21
, Corollary 6.5.6]
together with
[
LMM21
, Theorem 8.5.1]
and
[
MM23
, Theorem 5.0.1]
that the natural homomorphism
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})})
is
surjective
. Moreover, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 10.18
.

There is a canonical surjective homomorphism
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})})\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}
.

Proof.

It follows from the definitions that the action of
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
on
\mathcal{A}(X)
commutes with the
T_{X}
-action. Moreover, the action of
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
on
\mathcal{A}(X)
is locally finite.
We conclude that:

(10.4.3)

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}=\Big(\mathcal{A}(X)_{0}/\sum_{i>0}\mathcal{A}(X)_{-i}\mathcal{A}(X)_{i}\Big)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}=\mathcal{A}(X)_{0}^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}/\Big(\sum_{i>0}\mathcal{A}(X)_{-i}\mathcal{A}(X)_{i}\Big)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}

(10.4.4)

\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})})=\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}_{0}/\sum_{i>0}\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}_{-i}\mathcal{A}(X)^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}_{i}.

Clearly, (
10.4.4
) surjects onto (
10.4.3
).
∎

As a consequence, we always have a surjection
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}
. On the cohomological side, we also expect that the restriction homomorphism
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})^{A^{\vee}}
is always surjective (
Conjecture

10.10
). An evidence is given by
Lemma

10.9
.

Warning 10.19
.

It is crucial that we are passing to the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-fixed points. It is in general
not
true that the restriction homomorphism
H^{*}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{A^{\vee}}
is surjective.

This phenomenon has already appeared in the case where
A^{\vee}
acts trivially in
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
as follows. In
Lemma

10.3
, we have described the condition for
A^{\vee}
to act trivially on
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
and
\tilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is an orbit with normal closure. On the other hand, the necessary condition for the pullback
H^{*}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
to be surjective is much stricter, see
Theorem

B.1
and
Theorem

B.2
.

Remark 10.20
.

Conjecture

10.16
, or equivalently the
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-isomorphism (
10.4.1
), implies a bijection between the two
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-sets
\Con(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})/A^{\prime}
and the set of irreducible modules of
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X)
. When
e^{\vee}
is an even nilpotent element, we can identify
\Con(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})/A^{\prime}
with
\text{Irr}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})/A^{\prime}
where
\text{Irr}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
is the set of irreducible components of the Springer fiber
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
. This set
\text{Irr}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})/A^{\prime}
is conjectured to be the
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
-set that parametrizes finite dimensional simple modules with integral central character of
\mathcal{W}_{e^{\vee}}
, the W-algebra attached to
e^{\vee}
(
[
H24b
, Conjecture 1.2]
). In loc. cit., this conjecture has been checked for exceptional
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
. Therefore, it is desirable to establish a connection between the simple modules of
\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X)
and the modules of
\mathcal{W}_{e^{\vee}}
. This is a topic of ongoing work by the authors.

We now return to the case where
e^{\vee}
is distinguished. Note that in Conjecture
10.16
we tensor both sides by
\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm 1}]
. One still may expect that the isomorphism in this conjecture extends to
\hbar=0
. That would imply the identification

(10.4.5)

H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{A^{\vee}}\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}/(\hbar).

The RHS is a quotient of
\mathbb{C}[X^{T}]^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}
that, in its turn, is a quotient of
\mathbb{C}[(X/\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee}))^{T}]
(
Lemma

10.18
). Recall that
X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
and
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=\operatorname{Aut}_{D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}(\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee}))
. Assume also that the closure
\overline{D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}
is normal.
We have:

(X/\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee}))^{T}=(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]/{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})})^{T}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]^{T}=\overline{D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}\cap\mathfrak{h}^{*}.

It follows that the natural morphism
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X))^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}/(\hbar)
is surjective. So, the RHS of (
10.4.5
) is the quotient of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]
(after we assume the normality of
\overline{D(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})}
). On the cohomological side, we expect the restriction homomorphism
H^{*}(\mathcal{B})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e})^{A^{\vee}}
to be surjective for certain (but not all)
e^{\vee}
distinguished. Two particular cases where (
10.4.5
) holds are mentioned in Section 10.4.3.

In general, even for distinguished
e^{\vee}
, we do not expect that the specialization of
H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})^{A^{\vee}}
to
\hbar=0
is isomorphic to the specialization of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar}(X))
. However, regardless of
e^{\vee}
being distinguished or not, one still might expect that the
images

(10.4.6)

\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e})),~\operatorname{Im}(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar})^{\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})})

are isomorphic. We note that
\operatorname{Im}(H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e}))\subset H^{*}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e})^{A^{\vee}})
.

For
\hbar\neq 0
, homomorphisms in (
10.4.6
) should be surjective (the second one is known to be true by
[
LMM21
, Corollary 6.5.6]
),
so the identification of images should imply the conjectural identification (
10.4.2
) above.

10.4.2.
Case of arbitrary
e^{\vee}

Let us now assume that
e^{\vee}
is arbitrary.
Assume that
Y^{\vee}=\widetilde{S}(e^{\vee})
,
X=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})]
and let
\widetilde{X}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
X
(described in
[
LMM21
, Section 7.2]
, see also
[
Nam22
]
).
Let
\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}}
be the deformation of
\widetilde{X}
over
\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}
.
Explicitly, we have:

\widetilde{X}=G\times^{P}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}]\times\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}),~\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}}=G\times^{P}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}]\times[\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}]^{\perp}).

Set
X_{L}:=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{\mathbb{O}}_{L}]
.

For a module
M
over
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}},\hbar]
, we will denote by
M_{\hbar}
the
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}},\hbar^{\pm 1}]
-module
M\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}},\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}},\hbar^{\pm 1}]
. Recall that
L^{\vee}
is the connected component of
1\in Z_{G^{\vee}}(T_{e^{\vee}})
and recall that
e^{\vee}
is
distinguished
in
\mathfrak{l}^{\vee}=\operatorname{Lie}L^{\vee}
. Let
A_{L^{\vee}}
be the component group of
Z_{L^{\vee}}(e^{\vee})
. The group
A_{L^{\vee}}
acts naturally on
H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})
.
We make the following general conjecture (compare with Remark
8.8
).

Conjecture 10.21
.

We have an isomorphism of algebras:

(10.4.7)

\operatorname{Im}\left(H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(T^{*}\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}})_{\hbar}^{A^{\prime}_{L^{\vee}}}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Im}\left(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h},\hbar]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}]\rightarrow\Gamma(\widetilde{X}_{\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}}^{T_{X}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{X})))_{\hbar}\right).

Remark 10.22
.

Let us explain how Conjectures
10.16
and
10.21
are related. Assume that the Conjecture (
10.16
) holds for
(e^{\vee},L^{\vee})
. We expect the following isomorphism:

(10.4.8)

H^{*}_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}})^{A^{\prime}_{L^{\vee}}}_{\hbar}\simeq\Gamma(\widetilde{X}^{T_{X}}_{\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}},\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{e^{\vee}}}(\widetilde{X})))_{\hbar}.

Assuming Conjecture (
10.16
) holds, we expect that the identification of Conjecture
10.21
is compatible with the isomorphism (
10.4.8
).

Let us finish this section mentioning that we
do not
have any conjecture describing
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}}(X))
for
X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
. Informally, the reason is the following.

As we have already seen, the specialization of
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
to
\hbar=1
,
\lambda=0
should be isomorphic to
H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{\mathbb{C}^{\times}})^{A^{\prime}}
. Note also that for
\hbar=1
and
\lambda
being generic, the fiber
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{X}}(X))_{(\lambda,1)}
should be isomorphic to
(H^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{T_{e}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}))^{A_{L}^{\prime}}
.
So, to be able to describe the whole family
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}_{\hbar,\mathfrak{h}_{X}}(X))
via
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
, we need some “cohomology theory” that captures the
A^{\vee}
-action on
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
. It is not clear to us what the right candidate should be.

Note that Conjecture
10.21
bypasses this problem by considering the image of the equivariant cohomology of
\mathcal{B}
, this allows to neglect the actions of
A^{\vee}
and
\bar{A}_{L}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
on the targets.

10.4.3.
Evidence for the conjectures

Conjecture

10.16
is closer to the original Nakajima-Hikita conjecture in the sense that we keep the algebra
\mathcal{C}_{\nu}(\mathcal{A}(\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})))
on the right side.
On the other hand,

Conjecture

10.21
is a direct generalization of
Conjecture

8.4
for
X^{\vee}=S_{e^{\vee}}
,
X=\Spec(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})])
. Next, we explain some evidence for
Conjecture

10.16
and
Conjecture

10.21
when
e^{\vee}
is distinguished.

Consider the case where
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is of type D and the partition of
e^{\vee}
has two distinct parts. In this case, both
A^{\vee}
and
\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
are trivial. The identification
10.4.5
becomes the classical Hikita conjecture, which is true (see
[
H24a
, Proposition 6.1]
). A “twin” case is where
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
C
and the partition of
e^{\vee}
has two distinct parts. We now have
A^{\vee}=\bar{A}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, and
\widetilde{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
is a double cover of
{D}(\mathbb{O}^{\vee})
. The identification
10.4.5
is true by
[
H24a
, Remark 5.5]
. We also expect
Conjecture

10.21
to hold in this setting, the case where
e^{\vee}
is subregular in type D has been proved in
[
CHY24
, Theorem 1.3]
.

Remark 10.23
.

We note that by
[
HL14
]
, in this case,
X^{\vee}
can be studied as a quiver variety. The Coulomb branch of the corresponding quiver gauge theory is a slice in affine Grassmannian. We expect it to be isomorphic to the closure of the corresponding type
D
orbit, similar to
[
MVK22
]
.

Appendix A
Concrete examples of weight computations

Assume, for simplicity, that
\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathfrak{g}
is a Borel subalgebra. The statement of
Proposition

9.4
implies the coincidence of certain weights of
\mathfrak{h}
. Namely, consider the two different compositions
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]^{W_{M}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar]}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*},\hbar]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{[w]\in W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l}),\hbar]
. Specializing to the parameters
(\lambda,\hbar_{0})\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathfrak{l})\oplus\mathbb{C}
we get a collection of one-dimensional representations of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{h}^{*}]
. Using the top path in the diagram,
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
goes to the equivariant Chern class of the restriction of the line bundle
\mathfrak{L}(\chi)
on
T^{*}\mathcal{B}
to
(\widetilde{S}(\chi^{\vee})^{Z_{L^{\vee}}}
. Using the bottom path, we compute the image of
\chi
under the Cartan comoment map. In the example below we explicitly compute the sets of weights obtained both ways and confirm that they coincide.

Let
G^{\vee}
be
\operatorname{PGL}_{4}
. Consider the nilpotent element
e^{\vee}
of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
corresponding to the partition
\mathbf{p}^{\vee}=(3,1)
and
L^{\vee}=(\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{3})/\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Consider the map

(A.0.1)

H_{{T^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\rightarrow H_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}^{\vee})\rightarrow H_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}})\rightarrow H_{T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}}^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}^{T_{e^{\vee}}})=\oplus_{W/W_{L}}\mathbb{C}[a,\hbar].

Here
T_{e^{\vee}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, via
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\ni t\mapsto[\operatorname{diag}(t,1,1,1)]
. Let
a
be the natural coordinate of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{t}_{e^{\vee}}]
.
Let us explicitly describe
\mathcal{B}_{e^{\vee}}
. It has three irreducible components
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
, each of them is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Namely, let
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}
be the standard basis in
\mathbb{C}^{4}
. We have

e^{\vee}\colon v_{1}\mapsto 0,~v_{4}\mapsto v_{3}\mapsto v_{2}\mapsto 0.

Then,

C_{1}=\{\langle p_{1}v_{2}+q_{1}v_{1}\rangle\subset\langle v_{1},v_{2}\rangle\subset\langle v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}\,|\,[p_{1}:q_{1}]\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\},

C_{2}=\{\langle v_{2}\rangle\subset\langle v_{2},p_{2}v_{3}+q_{2}v_{1}\rangle\subset\langle v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}\,|\,[p_{2}:q_{2}]\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\},

C_{3}=\{\langle v_{2}\rangle\subset\langle v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\subset\langle v_{2},v_{3},p_{3}v_{4}+q_{3}v_{1}\rangle\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}\,|\,[p_{3}:q_{3}]\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\}

The action of
T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the following:

(A.0.2)

(a,t)\cdot[p_{1}:q_{1}]=[t^{2}p_{1}:aq_{1}],~(a,t)\cdot[p_{2}:q_{2}]=[p_{2}:aq_{2}],~(a,t)\cdot[p_{3}:q_{3}]=[t^{-2}p_{3}:aq_{3}].

We have four
T_{e^{\vee}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-fixed points:

o_{1}=[0_{1}:1_{1}],~o_{2}=[1_{1}:0_{1}]=[0_{2}:1_{2}],~o_{3}=[1_{2}:0_{2}]=[0_{3}:1_{3}],~o_{4}=[1_{3}:0_{3}].

The identification with
{}^{L^{\vee}}W
is as follows:

o_{1}\mapsto 1=:w_{1},~o_{2}\mapsto(12)=:w_{2},~o_{3}\mapsto(123)=:w_{3},~o_{4}\mapsto(1234)=:w_{4}.

Consider the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{i}
over
\mathcal{B}
which fiber over a flag
\{0\}=F_{0}\subset F_{1}\subset F_{2}\subset F_{3}\subset F_{4}=\mathbb{C}^{4}
is
F_{i}/F_{i-1}
. Note that
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is
not

\operatorname{PGL}_{4}
-equivariant. On the other hand,
\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{i+1}^{-1}
is
\operatorname{PGL}_{4}
-equivariant.

Similar to above, let
\mathbb{C}_{i}
be the one-dimensional representation of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, such that
t\cdot v=t^{i}v
. We see that as representations of
T_{e}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
we have the following.

\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}|_{o_{1}}=\mathbb{C}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-2},\,\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}|_{o_{2}}=\mathbb{C}_{-1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}|_{o_{3}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{2}^{-1}|_{o_{4}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},

\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{3}^{-1}|_{o_{1}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{3}^{-1}|_{o_{2}}=\mathbb{C}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{0},\,\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{3}^{-1}|_{o_{3}}=\mathbb{C}_{-1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{0},\,\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{3}^{-1}|_{o_{4}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},

\mathcal{L}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{4}^{-1}|_{o_{1}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{4}^{-1}|_{o_{2}}=\mathbb{C}_{0}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{4}^{-1}|_{o_{3}}=\mathbb{C}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{2},\,\mathcal{L}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{4}^{-1}|_{o_{4}}=\mathbb{C}_{-1}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{-2},

We conclude that

e_{1}=e_{11}-e_{22}\mapsto(a-2\hbar,-a+2\hbar,2\hbar,2\hbar),

e_{2}=e_{22}-e_{33}\mapsto(2\hbar,a,-a,2\hbar),

e_{3}=e_{33}-e_{44}\mapsto(2\hbar,2\hbar,a+2\hbar,-a-2\hbar).

Let us now describe the Cartan comoment map. Recall that the dual variety is
T^{*}(\operatorname{SL}_{4}/P)
, where
P
is the parabolic corresponding to the Levi
L=(\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{3})\cap\operatorname{SL}_{4}
. Clearly,
